- 2026-09-16: **Removed the Infinite Questions inline feed and the card wrapping "Next 25 questions".** Emci reported the correct "Next 25 questions" button sitting inside an old card titled "A few questions · 0 of 16 unanswered" ("Tap when you feel like it. Not today's card.") that ALSO rendered an inline question with "Skip this one" / "Skip the rest". **Source:** `QuestionsFold` (`src/components/questions-fold.tsx`) drew the card itself, and the question came from the persisted **Infinite Questions** pack system (`routeQuestions` → `fetchLatestQuestionPack`/`generateQuestionBatch`, `question_packs`/`question_items`) — **not** a leftover of the deleted "A faster pass" sweep and **not** a 16-question pool: the 16 is `TRAIT_AXES.length`, the same 16 axes the bank counts. **The actual defect:** the feed's JSX sat OUTSIDE the `fullProfileLocked ? <OngoingRoundFold/> : <bank>` ternary, so it rendered **unconditionally** — a finished profile got the round AND a second unrelated question feed in one box. (The first investigation pass reported the two as mutually exclusive branches; reading the JSX directly disproved that, which is the only reason the real cause was found.) **Removed:** the feed's whole UI (routed item, options, skip-this/skip-rest, the checkpoint/"Keep going" pause, empty states, the "Tell Sage more" load press), the card chrome (title + lede) and the collapsible `SettingsFold` path, all state/handlers behind it, and its pipeline — **`src/lib/questions/route.ts` and `src/lib/questions/copy.ts` deleted**, plus `fetchLatestQuestionPack`/`saveQuestionPack`/`skipQuestionItem`/`skipRestOfQuestionPack` (store.ts), `mergeCategoryPriority` (deferral.ts), `QuestionsKind`/`RouteQuestionsResult`/`RouteQuestionsInput`/`RouteQuestionsMe` (types.ts), the `QUESTIONS_LABEL` re-export (sage-copy.ts) and 4 dead styles. **"Next 25 questions" (`OngoingRoundFold`) and the 50-question bank are untouched, and so is the `FullProfileBanner`** — all pinned by assertions. **Deliberately LEFT dead-but-present:** `generateQuestionBatch`, `claimQuestionsBatch` and the `call-sites.ts` entry are now uncalled, but they are AI/quota plumbing and `check:ai` pins `AI_CALL_SITES.length === 10`; ripping them out is a quota-wiring change that needs emci's ok. **Three consequences emci must know:** (1) **the `?axis=` deep link is now IGNORED** — Explore, categories-fold, explore-panel, profile-fill-fold and sage-title-card still pass it and still land on Questions correctly, but nothing front-loads that axis, because the bank shows all 16 at once; (2) **the screen no longer reads the crisis flag** — crisis suppressed the FEED, inside `routeQuestions`; the bank and the round never read it, so this is inert, confirmed by review; (3) **the AI-consent gate on this screen is gone, and this is the one to actually decide.** `route.ts` gated generation on `ai_consent`; `runOngoingRound` **never did, before or after this change**, so no surviving path lost a gate — it is a **pre-existing hole**, not a regression. But consent-denied users used to at least see the denied copy here and now the screen says nothing about consent at all. **Escalated rather than fixed: wiring consent into `runOngoingRound` is new AI-consent work and needs emci's sign-off.** **Tests were inverted, not dropped:** ~400 lines of `routeQuestions` scenarios in `scripts/questions-check.ts` covered deleted code and went with it, replaced by deletion guards (files must stay deleted; 12 symbols must not reappear in the fold; "Next 25 questions" must survive) — and the same in `full-profile-signal-check.ts`, `ongoing-round-save-check.ts`, `wave19-check.ts`, `no-auto-ai-check.ts`. **The reviewer caught that the first version of that guard was VACUOUS and it took three attempts to fix** — `` `${gone}` `` inside a template literal is a literal BACKSPACE, not a word boundary, even though the source reads ``, so all 12 guards passed against anything forever. Replaced with a `codeOnly()` comment-stripper plus `String.includes`, and **proved non-vacuous with a negative control** (appending `const __probe = routeQuestions;` makes the check fail; removing it passes 25/25) — the lesson being that a deletion guard nobody has watched FAIL is not evidence of anything. **Unverified and left for emci's device pass:** the bank/round no longer sit on a `backgroundElement` surface and `styles.body` keeps its own horizontal padding on top of the screen's, so spacing may read slightly inset — a design call, not guessed at here. Verified: typecheck, eslint (only a pre-existing unused-disable in `OngoingRoundFold`), `check:questions` 25/25, `check:full-profile-signal` 6/6, `check:no-auto-ai` 10/10, `check:wave19` 16/16, `check:ongoing-round-save` 14/14, `check:ongoing-round` 6/6, `check:badges` 18/18, `check:ai` 17/17, `check:prewarm` 16/16, `check:bank-pool`, `check:reroll`, `check:chunked-generate`, `check:home-hydrate` 12/12, plus two reviewer passes (first FAIL on the vacuous guard, then PASS). **`check:ota-gate` not run, per emci's policy.**

- 2026-09-16: **Bug fixed — Home stayed locked after the 50th bank answer, until an app restart.** Emci reported finishing every bank question and Home not switching to its unlocked state ("Load insight" / "Load story"). **The gate was never wrong — the data behind it was stale.** `isFullProfileDone(tracks, bootstrapReady)` in `src/app/(tabs)/index.tsx` reads the one canonical signal correctly (confirmed: no divergent re-derivation, `check:full-profile-signal` green), but `reloadHome`/`fetchHomeBootstrap` ran in a **mount-keyed `useEffect`**, and Questions (`(tabs)/intake-sweep.tsx`) is a **sibling TAB route that expo-router keeps mounted** — so returning to Home never remounted it, `tracks` never refetched, and `fullProfileDone` held the locked value until the process restarted. Ruled out as causes: AI consent (orthogonal — it gates generation, not the screen), threshold drift (bank is 50, gate compares against the bank total), and a stale OTA (the whole Isolation Plan Cards A–G branch is unshipped, so no production user ever hit this). **Fix (client only — no schema, auth, env, or dependency change):** the mount effect became `useFocusEffect(useCallback(...))`. It covers mount too (Home is the first tab, so first focus *is* mount), so it **replaces** the old effect rather than adding a second fetch; `fetchHomeBootstrap` is one RPC read and spends no model call, so per-focus refetching stays free and Card B holds. **Two guards the reviewer required, both regressions the per-focus change itself would have introduced:** a failed refetch no longer flips `bootstrapFailed` for a user who already has good `tracks` (that "couldn't load" state is honest at cold open, but on a refocus blip it would replace a working screen mid-session — now tracked by a `loadedOnceRef`), and a `requestIdRef` sequence guard stops two quick focuses resolving out of order and letting an **older** response overwrite a newer one — which is the same stale-tracks class of bug being fixed here. `bootstrapReady` is deliberately **not** reset on refocus: flipping it false would make the gate return false for the length of the fetch and flash the locked state at a finished user every single time they came back. **Second half — the announcement banner.** New `src/components/full-profile-banner.tsx` ("Full profile enabled"), mounted on Questions and driven by the **same `isFullProfileDone` signal** as the Home unlock, so the banner and the unlock **can never disagree**: if it shows, Load insight / Load story are already live. It makes no model call (copy and a `View`), and shows once per account via the existing `src/lib/full-profile-unlock.ts` AsyncStorage flag — **not a `me` column, which would be a schema change needing emci's ok.** It replaces the `FullProfileUnlockAck` celebration that went with the parked milestone strip, which had left finishing the bank a silent event. **Note the string is NOT the one already in the repo** — `check-milestone-badge.tsx` still holds `FULL_PROFILE_UNLOCK_COPY = 'Full profile unlocked.'` (dev-only reachable via `theme-lab.tsx`); emci asked for "Full profile enabled" and got it, but **the two are unreconciled and share the one-shot flag**, so if that badge ever comes back it will burn the flag on a different condition (`isProfileSettled`) and the banner silently stops showing. **Accepted, one-time:** any account that already finished the bank sees the banner once on its next Questions visit. **Shipped across two commits because a second session was concurrently editing `intake-sweep.tsx`** — `245f598` (Home fix + the banner, deliberately unwired) then the wiring once `95b3d75` landed; nothing of the other session's work was touched or committed. Verified: typecheck, eslint (one pre-existing unrelated exhaustive-deps warning on the crisis effect, confirmed present beforehand), `check:full-profile-signal` 6/6, `check:home-hydrate` 12/12, `check:no-auto-ai` 10/10, `check:questions` 41/41, `check:full-profile` 8/8, two reviewer passes. **`check:ota-gate` deliberately not run, per the new policy emci set the same day.** Not device-verified — the on-device repro (answer the 50th question, tap Home, expect the unlocked state with no restart) is still emci's to run.

- 2026-09-15: **Answered-question / full-profile history reappeared after delete + re-signup — the DEVICE was never cleared, the server was always clean.** Emci reported a freshly created account showing the deleted account's answered questions and a "full profile unlocked" state. **Diagnosed against the LIVE database rather than by reading migrations, and that is what settled it:** an orphan scan over every user-keyed uuid column in `public` (`user_id`, `owner_id`, `sender_id`, `peer_id`, `user_a`, `user_b`) returned **zero** orphan rows; all 20 `account_deletions` rows report `rows_remaining = 0`; the account created **seven seconds** after the latest deletion holds 0 `trait_tracks`, 0 `question_packs`, 0 `question_items`, 0 `daily_insights` and 16 null trait columns. So it is neither "data not deleted" nor "new signup re-attaching to old rows" (each signup gets a fresh GoTrue uuid) — **the server had nothing left to show.** **Root cause:** `deleteAccount()` cleared the Supabase auth session and *nothing else*, so every other `ato.*` AsyncStorage key survived the deletion and the next signup on that device read it straight back. `ato.today-insight.v1` and the iOS widget App Group keys (`read`/`do`/`hasCard`) are the clearest case — they carry **no user id at all**, and Home paints from that cache on mount before any network call, so a brand-new account rendered the deleted account's insight and the home-screen widget kept showing it indefinitely. This is the same *class* of bug as the 2026-09-13 full-profile-unlock fix, one layer up: that fix scoped one key; this one establishes that clearing device state is part of deleting an account at all. **Fix (client only — no schema, no RLS, no auth, no env, no dependency change):** new `src/lib/local-account-data.ts` with `clearLocalAccountData()`, which is **deny-by-default** — it erases every `ato.*` key *except* an explicit keep-list of genuine device preferences (appearance, crisis region, push prefs/asked, AI provider override) and the `ato.auth.` prefix that `clearLocalSession` owns. **That direction is the load-bearing decision:** a key added later that turns out to hold account content is erased without anyone remembering to update the file, and the worst case of erasing one preference too many is a user re-picking a theme. It also resets the three warm in-memory caches (`answered-option-storage`, `category-page-position`, `full-profile-unlock`) — delete and re-signup happen in one app session with no relaunch, so clearing storage alone would leave those `Map`s answering for the previous account — and blanks + reloads the widget App Group, which no OTA can otherwise reach. Wired into `clearLocalSession` (covering delete-account, onboarding abandon, and the stale-session recovery in `use-session`) via a lazy dynamic import so `supabase.ts` does not pull the question/insight/widget modules into app start, and into the You-tab sign-out directly, since that path uses a global `signOut` rather than `clearLocalSession`. **A plain sign-out bled exactly the same way a deletion did — that is now closed too.** Second layer of defence: `CachedInsight` gained a `userId`, `loadCachedInsight(expectedUserId?)` refuses a payload owned by someone else (legacy unowned payloads included), and Home passes its signed-in id; `push.ts` deliberately still calls it unscoped, which is safe now the key cannot outlive its account. **Manual-verification hook (house convention):** a Dev Lab **Local account data** panel lists the account-scoped keys on the device — names only, never values, since some hold generated personal content — with a wipe button, so the device can be *seen* clean before a delete and after the next signup instead of being assumed clean. New `scripts/local-account-data-check.ts` + `check:local-account-data`; **gate is now 77.** Its load-bearing assertion is that `isAccountScopedKey` stays deny-by-default: inverted into an allow-list, a later key silently survives deletion and the bug returns quietly, on a two-account device nobody tests. Verified: `npm run check:ota-gate` — typecheck + lint + all 77 offline checks green. **Reviewer caught a leak I had missed, and it was the worst one:** the OS-level push schedules are neither AsyncStorage nor the widget, and `morningPush(insight.title)` is a **DAILY REPEATING** notification carrying AI-written text about the account being deleted. `PushRuntime` unmounts on sign-out, so nothing cancelled them — the deleted account's insight kept firing on that phone until some *later* account finished onboarding and reached `resyncPushForUser`, or forever if nobody signed up on that device again. New `cancelAllScheduledPush()` in `push.ts` (split out of `syncPushSchedule`'s opening cancel block, which only cancels in order to immediately reschedule), called from `clearLocalAccountData`. Reviewer also caught that the ownership check was bypassed during session restore — `useSession` resolves asynchronously, so Home's first renders have no user id and an `undefined`-means-don't-check hook painted the cache unchecked during exactly the window the check exists for; `useDailyInsight` now holds at null until the id is known, costing a frame on cold start instead of briefly showing one account another's text. **Accepted minors, not fixed:** an abandoned onboarding now loses `ato.pending_invite_code` and the code must be retyped (deny-by-default is worth more than that friction); a transient offline launch that makes `getUser()` error still routes through `clearLocalSession` and now erases the display-hint keys too (pre-existing trigger, cosmetic loss, real data is server-side); and one-time after this ships, a pre-existing unowned cached insight reads as null so Home is briefly empty until the first refetch. **wave72 APPLIED and verified live (2026-09-15)** — confirmed against the database after emci applied it: `count_user_rows` references all three tables, returns 7 for a live account and **0 for a non-existent one**, which is the exact value `delete-account` reports as a clean deletion. (a) `supabase/migrations/wave72_count_user_rows_dev_tables.sql` it adds `dev_access_grants` / `dev_trace_sessions` / `dev_trace_events` to `count_user_rows()`. **Audit completeness only, not a leak:** all three already cascade, which is why the orphan scan is clean; they were simply missing from the hardcoded list, so `rows_remaining` can under-count — the same false-clean-audit failure wave67 existed to prevent. (b) It deliberately does **not** add `category_question_batches` / `category_question_items`, which an earlier read flagged as the same gap: `to_regclass` returns **NULL** for both against the live database — **wave44 was never applied**, the tables do not exist, and naming them would make the function fail to create. If wave44 is ever applied, both must join the list in that same pass.

- 2026-09-14: **Shared question pool moved ahead of demand — per-user dedup, depth measurement, background prewarm (`wave68`).** Emci asked to plan a shift from on-demand generation to a pre-generated shared reserve pool of 25-question batches ("keep 3 batches in reserve, refill below threshold"). **The investigation reframed the premise, and that reframing is the important part: `question_bank_pool` (wave49) was ALREADY a shared, not per-user, pool, and it does not deplete** — serving a question only bumps `times_served`, it removes nothing. So "3 batches in reserve, drawn down by demand" does not describe the mechanics, and bursty demand cannot exhaust it. The two things that were genuinely broken: (1) **latency** — AI fallback ran synchronously inside the user's round-start against a 40s budget, which is why `MAX_SHORTFALL_RETRIES` had already been cut 2→1; (2) **per-user freshness** — `fetchBankCandidates` never joined against the user's own answer history, so a bank question could be re-served to the same person once it aged out of the bounded ~25-30 recent-TEXT window. On a pool targeting only ~100 rows, that got worse the more rounds someone played. **Shipped (emci approved steps 1/2/4 then 3a; pg_cron option 3b explicitly NOT built — no background-job mechanism exists in this project and a service-role identity would bypass `claim_ai_call`, a hard invariant):** `wave68` adds two read-only security-definer RPCs — `fetch_bank_candidates` (draw, now anti-joined against `question_items` as well as `question_bank_reroll_exclusions`) and `bank_pool_depth` (per-axis count of what this user has never seen; nothing could measure this before) — plus a partial index on `question_items (user_id, question_bank_item_id)`. **No new tables, no new columns, no write-path or RLS change.** Client: `prewarm.ts` (pure) derives a per-axis reserve target from the tiered axis plan — **reserve is expressed in ROUNDS of cover, not a flat pool size, because axes have different per-round demand (3/round vs 1/round)**; `run-prewarm.ts` tops up the shared bank only (never per-user rows), fire-and-forget after a round is served, in-process guard + 6h per-device cooldown stamped BEFORE generation so a failed pass can't retry immediately, capped at `PREWARM_MAX_QUESTIONS`=10 per pass (each 5 questions = one `ai-generate` call on the triggering user's quota). **Skip semantics, checked at emci's request and found HALF wrong:** the server (`claim_ongoing_round_complete`, wave52) already requires all 25 answered and ignores `skipped_at`, but the client's `isOpenQuestionItem` treats a skip as resolved. Fixed by giving the round path its own answer-only predicate (`isUnansweredQuestionItem`/`roundFullyAnswered`); Infinite Questions deliberately keeps skip-is-resolved. **Latent, not live** — the round pager has no skip control today, only reroll; pinned so adding one later can't reintroduce the mismatch. Verified: typecheck + lint clean, new `check:prewarm` 16/16, `check:ongoing-round-save` 14/14, `check:reroll` 15/15, `check:ongoing-round` 6/6, `check:questions` 41/41, reviewer pass. Gate green except the known pre-existing `dev-test-user` failure (35/35 when the uncommitted edits are stashed — not touched, not committed). **Two known gaps, both deliberate, both open:** (a) **`reroll_question_item` (wave54) still excludes only the reroll list + same pack**, so the precheck is now stricter than the effect RPC — fail-safe (subset, can never green-light a spend the RPC fails) but it means a reroll can still hand back a question from one of this user's own earlier rounds, and `no_candidates` appears sooner on a thin axis. Closing it needs the same anti-join inside that RPC. (b) **The first round after this ships will be slow for an existing account** — most of the ~100-row pool is already in their `question_items`, so bank yield collapses and composition falls through to AI under the 40s budget; prewarm can't help because it only fires after a successful start. Post-reset this is a non-issue; pre-reset, expect one slow round per existing account. **DEPLOY ORDER: apply `wave68` BEFORE publishing any OTA** — `bank-pool.ts` now hard-depends on `fetch_bank_candidates` with no fallback.

- 2026-09-14: **Ongoing-round questions could silently ship short of 25.** Traced from a broader question-generation audit with emci: `fillAxisCountsChunked` (`chunked-generate.ts`) retries an AI shortfall once (deliberately capped, to stay inside the client's 25s round-start timeout), then gives up and leaves the slot unfilled with no error — a round could quietly land at e.g. 18-24 questions instead of 25. **Fix:** `composeOngoingRound` (`ongoing-round.ts`) now makes one more pass at the shared `question_bank_pool` (reusing the existing `fillFromBank` helper) for whatever axes are still short after the AI attempt, before returning — no extra AI call, no extra latency. If even the bank has nothing for that axis, it now at least logs the shortfall instead of failing silently. New test in `scripts/ongoing-round-check.ts` simulates a persistent AI failure on one axis and confirms the round still lands on the full 25. Verified: typecheck, `check:questions` (41/41), `check:ongoing-round` (6/6), `check:ongoing-round-save` (14/14), lint clean, reviewer pass.

- 2026-09-14: **Account deletion audit — `delete-account` itself is clean; its self-check was stale, now fixed.** Emci reported deletion "doesn't fully clear the user." Investigated every user-scoped table across all migrations: `auth.admin.deleteUser()` + `ON DELETE CASCADE` on every FK genuinely clears everything (checks, trait_tracks/history, tokens, Circle/connections, invites, explore/question data, etc.) — no leftover rows. The only real bug: `count_user_rows()` (`stage8_invite_referral.sql`), which `delete-account` calls as its own in-band proof (`rows_remaining` in the `account_deletions` audit row), predated wave19+ and was missing every table added since — cosmetic under-reporting only, not an actual leak. Cross-referenced against the known 2026-09-13 re-registration/full-unlock bug (per-device AsyncStorage flag) — confirmed unrelated and already fixed; no evidence of a new repro. **Fix:** `supabase/migrations/wave67_count_user_rows_refresh.sql` — refreshed `count_user_rows()` to the current table list (mirrors `reset_dev_test_user()` in `wave66_dev_test_user_reset.sql`). Applied live by emci. No change to `delete-account/index.ts` itself. `npm run check:ota-gate` green for this change (one unrelated pre-existing failure on uncommitted `dev-test-user.ts` edits, not touched).

- 2026-09-13: **Bug fixed — full-profile-unlock celebration stayed suppressed after delete-account + re-register.** Emci reported: delete account, re-register, 50-question intake "doesn't show after onboarding" and "doesn't unlock" after completing it. Investigated (server-side deletion, cascades, `complete_signup`, trait_tracks) — all clean, confirmed via `delete-account` edge function's own in-band `rows_remaining` proof and `complete_signup` keying strictly on a fresh `auth.uid()`. **Root cause was purely client-side:** `src/lib/full-profile-unlock.ts` stored the "have I shown the unlock celebration" flag under one global AsyncStorage key with no user id, so once any account on a device triggered it, every later account on that same device — including a freshly re-registered one — silently skipped the celebration reveal (`check-milestone-badge.tsx`'s `fullPicture`/badge-unlock state itself was always correct and live; only the one-time reveal was gated wrong). Confirmed with emci directly that the Questions tab itself was never broken (50 questions render and answer fine when opened manually) — this was one bug, not two. **Fix:** scoped the AsyncStorage key per account id (`ato.profile.fullUnlock.seen.<userId>.v1`), same pattern `questions-fold.tsx`/`answered-option-storage.ts`/`category-page-position.ts` already use, in-memory cache changed from a single boolean to a `Map<string, boolean>` keyed by userId. Updated the one caller (`check-milestone-badge.tsx`) and `scripts/badges-check.ts`'s assertions (19/19 checks pass, gate: typecheck clean). **Side effect, called out by review, accepted:** every account that already saw the celebration under the old global key will see it once more after this ships, since the old key is orphaned and each account's new per-id key starts unset — one-time, harmless, no migration needed.

- 2026-09-13: **+21 ATO token-payout gap fixed — turned out broader than originally scoped.** Follow-up to the "deprioritize until after reset" decision earlier the same day: investigated instead of waiting, and the scope was wrong. Original framing: only affects users who touched the optional scenario phase. **Actual finding, verified directly in code (`me.ts:523-529`, `trait-history.ts:22-38`):** once ANY direct trait source (grid intake `self_slider`, ranking taps `self_tap`, Settings edits `self_settings`, Does-Sage-know-you confirms) owns an axis, `shouldWriteReportTrack` permanently blocks every later inferred (`self_situation`) write on that axis from moving the number — `collectAnswers` correctly downgrades it to `countOnly: true`, but `persistMergedTraits` then skipped `trait_history` entirely for count-only answers (no value change = nothing for `historyDiff` to see), so an axis captured directly early on could never again contribute toward `claim_full_profile_complete`'s ≥50-row floor, no matter how many times it was re-answered on the Questions tab. This affects any real user who touches ANY direct-input surface before finishing all 50 questions — not an edge case. **Fix (emci's explicit choice: count these toward the floor, not lower the threshold or park it):** count-only `self_situation` answers now get an explicit `trait_history` row in `persistMergedTraits` (`src/lib/me.ts`), mirroring the existing `self_game` explicit-row pattern just above it in the same function — same value as before (no number movement claimed), just recording that the answer happened. Deliberately scoped narrow: does NOT touch `historyDiff`'s separate value-unchanged dedup for genuinely-inferred writes elsewhere in the app (a real, distinct, smaller issue — an inferred write that doesn't move the rounded value by more than 2 decimals still produces no history row anywhere in the app, not just here) — flagged but not bundled into this fix, since fixing that more broadly changes visible trait-history-timeline behavior everywhere, not just the payout path, and wasn't part of what emci approved. Gate: typecheck + lint clean, `check:questions` 41/41, `check:trait-sources` + `check:traits` (33/33) all green. **Not live-verified** — no real account has been run through 50 real Questions-tab answers to confirm the payout actually lands; that needs an actual test account and is a good candidate for the closing device-test milestone. No schema/migration change — the RPC's counting logic (`claim_full_profile_complete`, wave52) is untouched; this is entirely a client-side write-path fix.

- 2026-09-13: **New chat, execution phase — foundational health check passed, screen-by-screen scaffold pass done for 10 of 11 screens (Dev Tools spot-checked only), one bug found and fixed on Home.** Full detail in `docs/NOW.md`'s "On" section. Short version: auth/session, MeContext, nav bar all healthy; Home/Explore/Sage/You/Around/Circle/Categories/Legends/Roll/Public Profile all match `docs/screen-map.md`, nothing tagged `[not built yet]`/`[blocked on shared fix]`. Fixed the already-documented Sunday duplicate week-row bug on Home (`src/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`) — reviewer caught that `docs/ATO_DEVICE_TESTS.md` documented the opposite resolution, a real product-direction fork; emci decided to keep the always-visible "This week" row and drop the Sunday-only slot row. Doc updated to match. No shared-fix backlog — Phase 3 of the scaffold-first plan has nothing queued from this pass.

- 2026-09-13: **Group-1 (no-OTA-needed) maintenance pass — 3 of 9 items done, 3 blocked on values only emci can choose, 3 deliberately not done.** **Done:** (1) `parse.ts` silent-catch logging — both bare `catch { return []; }` around `JSON.parse` (`parseQuestionBatch`, `parseQuestionSweep`) now call a new `logParseFailure` helper, matching `logDrop`'s convention; logs the parser name and raw length only, never the untrusted model text. `check:questions` 41/41, typecheck + eslint clean. (2) Stale EAS production env vars deleted (`EXPO_PUBLIC_GEMINI_API_KEY`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_GEMINI_MODEL`). (3) `ai-generate` Edge Function redeployed (now version 9), making commit `6f6bb61`'s ongoing-round timeout fix actually live. **Two stale doc claims corrected in the process, one of which was dangerous:** (a) `docs/NOW.md` said the EAS work "needs an interactive EAS login; could not be run from the build session" — **false**, both the EAS and Supabase CLIs on this machine are authenticated and run fine non-interactively (`eas env:*`, `supabase secrets list`, `supabase functions deploy` all verified working); (b) the same entry's "nothing under `src/` reads them" was written as blanket cover for the whole `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` model/provider family, but **`EXPO_PUBLIC_MODEL_PROVIDER` is still read live** at `src/lib/ai/config.ts:58` and `src/lib/voice/config.ts:26` — following the doc literally would have deleted a var that changes provider selection in production builds. It was kept. **Blocked on emci (a value only they can choose — deliberately not invented):** `DEV_UNLOCK_PASSWORD` secret + `dev-unlock` deploy (confirmed: the secret is absent from `supabase secrets list`, and `dev-unlock` is absent from the deployed-functions list, so this is genuinely outstanding); QA override invite-code rotation (`app_secrets`, currently 4 chars); dev-test account password rotation. **Deliberately NOT done:** +21 token-payout re-verification (emci's own Sep 13 decision was to re-verify *after* the pre-launch reset, which has not happened — testing now measures nothing); Claude/Grok `/ai-lab` check (confirmed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`XAI_API_KEY` absent from secrets — unset by choice, nothing to check); **pre-launch re-gating checklist items 2,3,4,6,7,8,9,11 — refused as actively harmful right now.** Those guards are `PRE_LAUNCH_DEV` (a runtime const that ships in the bundle) rather than `__DEV__` (compiled out of release JS) *on purpose* — flipping them today would strip dev tooling from emci's own TestFlight build, including the `push-test-card` needed for notification testing, and would immediately fail `check:prod-you`, whose docblock reads "Pre-launch: the production bundle MUST ship the You-tab crash/push probes so they work over OTA." That checklist is launch-day work, not maintenance. **Correction, same day, caught by a live-AI check:** the dev-test account password reset (below) did NOT actually take effect — a `check:card-live` run with the old `ATO-dev-user-2026` password still authenticated successfully, and that same run confirmed `ai-generate` v9 is genuinely healthy (real Gemini output, not bank content). The password rotation needs to be re-attempted and re-verified; see `docs/NOW.md`'s Left section. **Later the same day, on emci's go-ahead to generate the three secret values:** `DEV_UNLOCK_PASSWORD` was generated (crypto-random, 20 chars) and set, and `dev-unlock` was deployed — both verified present via `supabase secrets list` / `functions list`, `check:dev-unlock` 7/7. **The value is deliberately lowercase:** `dev-unlock-gate.tsx` sets `autoCapitalize="none"` and the function's `timingSafeEqual` compares exactly (no trim, no case-fold), so an uppercase secret would have meant 20 manual shift presses on a phone keyboard every unlock. The remaining two (QA override invite code, dev-test account password) could **not** be done from here: the Supabase CLI has no arbitrary-SQL subcommand (only `diff`/`dump`/`push`/`pull`/`reset`), and putting a secret in a migration file to use `db push` would commit a credential to git — exactly what `wave42`'s own comment forbids. Both were handed to emci as a one-line SQL paste and a dashboard action respectively, with generated values. Note for the invite code: `assert_invite_usable`/`complete_signup` both compare `upper(btrim(...))`, so it is case-insensitive and whitespace-tolerant — testers can type it either case. **Superseded within the hour, deliberately:** emci replaced all three generated values with one short memorable password and a dictionary-word invite code, reasoning "i dont care who can guess it i can switch this later." That is a defensible pre-launch tradeoff (invite-only, zero live users, full reset planned), and it was applied — but it converts all three from "strong secrets" into **hard blockers on the public-launch checklist**, so re-gating item 12 was rewritten to say so in capitals rather than leaving it as a soft "rotate before launch" note. The exposure that actually matters today is the invite code, not the passwords: `assert_invite_usable` is anon-callable with **no rate limiting**, so a guessable override code is the only thing between a stranger and a signup.

- 2026-09-13: **T-06/Stage 8 device pass marked fully confirmed; +21 ATO token-payout gap deprioritized (no migration/backfill now).** (1) T-06 (EAS binary 10 install/confirm + Sentry native crash symbolication) is confirmed passed on a real device — no remaining device-testing caveats; `docs/NOW.md`'s Stage 8 and Left sections updated to reflect this. (2) The `claim_full_profile_complete` (wave52) +21 ATO token-payout gap for scenario-phase users — documented 2026-09-11 as a real, still-open bug (count-only trait writes from the scenario-phase fix never emit a `trait_history` row, so those users can unlock Sage/Legends but never clear the RPC's ≥50-row floor to receive the token payout) — is **deprioritized, not fixed**: emci's call is that there are no live users yet, and everyone gets reset before launch, so there is nothing to migrate or backfill right now. **Do not close this out entirely** — after the pre-launch reset, re-verify that `claim_full_profile_complete` actually pays out correctly for a user who completes the full 50-question intake normally. If it still doesn't pay out post-reset, the root cause (`historyDiff` only emitting a row on value change, so a count-only write is invisible to the payout gate) is a real bug independent of any pre-launch test data and needs an actual fix, not just a reset. Docs-only, no code touched.

- 2026-09-13: **Device verification confirmed — OTA group `4bbf01ea` (notification prefs, evening/insight push rework, Categories' own route) manually tested by emci on TestFlight, no issues found.** No action needed; logged for the record per emci's explicit request. This closes the "not device-verified" caveat on the 2026-09-12 entry below and in `docs/NOW.md`.

- 2026-09-12: **Field inventory → judgment pass → build, on the notification/Categories/dead-weight questions.** Three-stage pass this session: (1) `docs/field-inventory.md` + `docs/screen-map.md` (all 17 screens, grep-verified) + `docs/dead-weight.md` + `docs/built-not-connected.md` — pure cataloging, no dead DB fields confirmed (everything flagged "loaded, not rendered" turned out used elsewhere on cross-check), one genuinely buried feature (`access-requests.ts`, dev-lab only), one real schema gap (`public_profile` RPC has no migration, same as the 4 baseline tables). (2) `docs/judgment-pass.md` — an Opus pass that researched Co-Star/Chani/The Pattern/Way of Life/Streaks before making calls, specifically **against** emci's own idea of randomized-throughout-the-day category notifications: ATO already sends ~15/week, the research threshold where ~46% of users disable notifications is 2-5/week, so adding volume on top would be the worst move; recommended cutting the (content-free) evening push and replacing the freed slots with a *smart-picked*, not random, category insight. (3) Built exactly that, approved plan: notification-prefs fold (You tab, device-local), evening cut to 3x/week skip-if-logged, 2x/week insight push (spotlight → stability → oldest-touched ranking, relays an existing `category_statements` row, never generates), Categories moved to its own hidden route off Explore's fold-stack, `around-lab.tsx` going/age-gate dev coverage, one `GOTCHAS.md` line. **Reviewer caught the load-bearing bug before push:** the first implementation scheduled evening/insight only for "tonight" and cancelled if the hour had passed — meaning the push would go silently dead for anyone who didn't open the app that exact day, which is backwards for a feature whose whole point is reminding people who haven't opened the app. Fixed to find the next matching weekday up to a week out (one-shot `DATE` trigger, recomputed every sync). Reviewer also caught: prefs toggling didn't resync (turning a kind off didn't cancel it until some unrelated event fired), the insight ranking used axis *value* not *stability* (opposite of the stated design), no fallback to the next-ranked category when the top one lacked a statement yet, and a timezone mismatch between the new weekday logic (device-local) and the existing logged-today check (`me.timezone`) — all fixed. **One thing decided conservatively rather than unilaterally:** the insight push relays `category_statements` text, which ships in-app regardless of `CATEGORY_STATEMENTS_COPY_REVIEWED` (existing pattern) — but a lock-screen push is a more prominent surface than an in-app screen, so `pickInsightPayload` returns null (push silently doesn't fire) while that flag stays false, rather than shipping unreviewed diagnosis-adjacent copy to the lock screen by default. Flip the flag once the copy's been read. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks green; **not device-verified** (push-notification timing/delivery can't be confirmed off-device). The live schema dump for the dead-weight column audit (`me`'s ~70 columns) is still pending from emci separately — a partial dump received mid-session (access_requests → dev_trace_events, alphabetical) confirmed every assumption already made (`checks.source` NOT NULL, `category_statements.superseded_at` nullable) but didn't yet include `me`/`public_profile`.

- 2026-09-11: **Dev-only intake-stage seeding — built in-app, not as a CLI, and scenarios-only deliberately seeds 16/50 rather than 0/50.** Emci asked for a way to jump a test account to any onboarding/intake state without filling forms, gated so it can never reach production. **Chose the in-app Dev Lab panel over a CLI script**: every existing `scripts/*-check.ts` is a pure/static assertion and nothing in `scripts/` has ever held a service-role key, so a CLI seeder would have meant introducing a new Supabase secret and a new pattern — while `dev-test-user.ts` + `dev-lab.tsx` already had the exact shape needed (`PRE_LAUNCH_DEV` + `DEV_TEST_USER_ID`, direct `me` writes plus `upsertTraitTracks`). **No schema change was needed** because intake progress is never stored: `bankTotalProgress` derives it live from per-axis report-track `answer_count`, so seeding N answers is just writing counts that sum to N under each axis's bank cap, and the live `sageUnlocked`/`legendsUnlocked` predicates then read the seeded state normally — nothing stubbed, no fake `question_items`. **The 0/50 vs 16/50 call:** the spec said "scenario phase only = 16 axes locked direct, 0/50 intake", but `self_scenario` is a direct source and `trackKindForSource` routes it to the report track, so all 16 of the optional phase's axis writes bump `answer_count` — a real account that finishes the scenarios and nothing else sits at **16/50**, and 0/50 is unreachable. Seeded 16 so the stage tests a state a user can actually be in; still well under the Sage threshold of 25, which is what the stage is for. **The pure half was split into `src/lib/dev-intake-stages.ts` (supabase-free)** specifically so `check:dev-test-user` can import and run the real plan/writer functions against the real thresholds instead of mirroring their arithmetic — an earlier draft mirrored them in the check, which would have passed a regression that wrote counts to the wrong track. **Accepted limit, do not "fix" by deleting the `me` row:** "Fresh signup" cannot reopen the "Introduce yourself" form, because that screen renders only under `guard={isAuthed && !hasMe}` in `_layout.tsx`, and the `me` row *is* the `@atodev` identity (the client also has no delete grant on it). The stage resets what the forms wrote instead. `celebrated_milestone_ids` is cleared on **every** stage, not just fresh, so milestone celebrations re-fire when jumping back down. **Preset 6 built same day, emci approved the auth/schema change:** `wave65_dev_collision_account.sql` provisions a second `auth.users` row (`@atodev2`, id `...d3e1`) and a hidden `me` row (`visible = false`), mirroring `wave31`'s shape but deliberately never sign-in-able — no `encrypted_password`, no `auth.identities` row. **Reviewer caught three things worth recording:** (1) the header originally overclaimed that the missing identities row alone blocks OTP/magic-link sign-in — reworded to name the real barrier (the undeliverable `@example.com` address) and warn not to rely on the identities gap; (2) every `me` insert fires `me_after_insert_issue_codes`, minting 4 real invite codes on an account nobody can sign into — harmless but now documented rather than a silent surprise; (3) `wave65` was missing the stale-email guard `wave31` has (`delete ... where email = ... and id <> ...`), which `on conflict (id)` alone does not cover — added. The read-only collision-check panel calls `checkHandleAvailable` (the production client path), not the bare `handle_taken` RPC, and distinguishes an actually-taken response from a format/network error via the returned message — an early draft treated any non-`ok` result as "taken," which would have shown a false-positive "fixed" on a network error. `check:dev-test-user` 25 → 30; the collision id is parsed out of the migration's stale-email guard specifically (not "the first quoted 36-char string in the file," which could silently drift to the wrong value on a reorder) and compared against `DEV_TEST_USER_ID` parsed the same way. Migration not yet applied to the live project — the panel will read "available" until it is.

- 2026-09-11: **Bug fix — `parseQuestionDraft` silently discarded AI questions, so rounds came up short with nothing in the logs.** Logged as a known gap in the Sage/Legends entry below; fixed this pass. Root cause: `parseQuestionDraft` (`src/lib/questions/parse.ts`) returned `null` on every reject path — prompt over 400 chars, fewer than 2 or more than 3 valid options, unknown axis, non-array options — with no log at all, so a batch that asked for 5 questions could return 3 and neither the caller nor Sentry saw a reason. **Fixed two ways:** (1) an over-400-char prompt is now **truncated instead of dropped**, cut at the last word boundary (`truncatePrompt`), with an explicit guard that strips a stranded high surrogate when a space-free prompt has to cut by index — a lone surrogate would make PostgREST reject the entire pack insert, not just one draft. 400 is **not** an arbitrary constant: it mirrors the DB CHECK on `question.prompt` (`wave17_infinite_questions.sql:188`, also `left(...,400)` at :260) and the comment at `bank-pool-match.ts:19`, so a truncated prompt still inserts; output is provably 1..400 JS chars, and Postgres `char_length` counts code points so it is always <= that. (2) Every remaining drop path logs via `logDrop` with the reason, the axis and the **original** prompt length; `parseQuestionBatch` logs when it finishes short of the requested count, and `parseQuestionSweep` logs only when items were genuinely malformed (the dedupe and `TRAIT_AXES` cap are normal and stay quiet). **Logs carry lengths and axis only, never prompt text**, and the unknown-axis value is sliced to 40 chars — untrusted model output must not be dumped whole. **`category` is deliberately not logged:** `QuestionDraft.category` (`types.ts:32`) is only ever set by the static bank, never by AI drafts, so axis is the only identity available — and when the axis itself is the malformed field it logs `unknown`. Convention note: `console.log('[questions] …')`, matching the 33 `console.log` vs 1 `console.warn` in `src/lib`. Verified: typecheck clean, lint clean on the file, `check:questions` 41/41, plus a throwaway probe confirming a 496-char prompt is kept and truncated to 398 at a word boundary, a 1-option draft is dropped **with** a log, and emoji / space-free / emoji-heavy prompts all come out 1..400 with no lone surrogate. One review round, PASS with no critical findings; three of its warnings (surrogate guard, axis slice, false-positive sweep log) were taken before push. `parse.ts` only — pagination and the handle/Legends fixes untouched, as scoped. **Not device-verified**; no schema, auth, env or dependency changes. **Still open, same audit, not fixed here:** the two `JSON.parse` catch blocks in this file still `return []` silently on a wholly malformed model response, and the duplicated intake-pager / ongoing-round implementations noted below.

- 2026-09-11: **Bug fix — `checkHandleAvailable` falsely reported taken handles as available for hidden/paused accounts; fixed at the source with a new `handle_taken` RPC (migration `wave64`).** Root cause: `checkHandleAvailable` (`src/lib/me.ts:821`) asked the `public_profile` RPC and read "no rows" as "handle available". `public_profile` is deliberately visibility-filtered (`stage8_invite_referral.sql:12` — "Hidden: public_profile does not return it"), so a handle owned by a paused/hidden account came back free; the user only failed at the end, inside `complete_signup`'s insert, on the `me.handle` unique constraint (23505 → `errorMessageForHandle`). **Fixed at the source, not patched around:** new `wave64_handle_taken_rpc.sql` adds `public.handle_taken(p_handle text) returns boolean` — `language sql`, `stable`, `security definer`, `set search_path = public` — doing `exists(select 1 from public.me where lower(me.handle) = lower(btrim(coalesce(p_handle,''))))`, i.e. real uniqueness regardless of visibility. `checkHandleAvailable` now calls it and treats `data === true` as taken; the old `Array.isArray(data)` row-shape juggling is **removed, not kept as a fallback** (no production users, per emci). **Grant is `authenticated` only, deliberately NOT anon** — unlike `assert_invite_usable` (`stage8_invite_referral.sql:313-314`), whose anon grant exists because it runs on the Auth screen pre-OTP. Onboarding's account step is post-OTP: `_layout.tsx:76-78` mounts it under `Stack.Protected guard={isAuthed && !hasMe}` and `isAuthed` comes from `useSession`, which validates the JWT via `auth.getUser()`. An anon grant would let an unauthenticated caller probe the existence of hidden/paused handles — exactly what `public_profile` conceals. A dead session fails closed to "Couldn't check that handle. Try again.", never a false "available". **Other `public_profile` callers left alone on purpose** (`src/app/[handle].tsx:39`, `src/lib/circle.ts:74`) — those legitimately want the filtered view so hidden users stay unscannable. **One real miss caught in review round 1:** `scripts/intake-check.ts:95` asserted `/public_profile/` against `me.ts` and was passing only because the new doc comment contained that string — the guard had silently stopped verifying anything and a regression would have shipped clean. Now `/rpc\('handle_taken'/`, which only the call site can satisfy. **Known gaps accepted, not fixed:** no functional index on `lower(handle)` so this is a seq scan on `public.me` (negligible at current row counts); the `me.handle` unique constraint cited as the race guard is **not in tracked migrations** — `public.me`'s DDL, RLS and `public_profile` itself predate `supabase/migrations/` and live only in the live DB, so 23505 is assumed, not verified from source. Gate: typecheck + lint + **all 74 offline checks** green, `check:intake` 19/19. Two review rounds — first FAIL (the stale `intake-check` literal), then PASS. **Not device-verified and DB-behaviour not yet confirmed:** the migration is applied by hand in the Supabase SQL editor and **must be applied before any OTA**, or every handle check fails closed. The one case to confirm live: a handle owned by a hidden/paused account now reads as taken. No auth, dependency, env or table-schema changes — one new function only.

- 2026-09-11: **Rename — `CategoryPagedQuestions` → `PagedQuestions` (`src/components/paged-questions.tsx`); cosmetic only, no behaviour change.** Prompted by a read-only audit asking whether the component was dead code left over from the removed Categorize Q&A system. It is not: it is the live, load-bearing pager for the frozen 50-question intake (`/intake-sweep` → `QuestionsFold` → `PagedQuestions`, fed by `bankProgressForAxis` off `QUESTIONS_BANK`), reachable from ~10 screens. The name was the only stale thing: commit `60d7c34` rewrote it from a category-per-screen layout into a flat 5-per-page book pager, so "Category" no longer described it and the `categories` prop now only supplies axis order (`uniqueCategoryAxes`). Renamed the component + file, its check script (`scripts/paged-questions-check.ts`, npm `check:paged-questions` — the gate discovers `check:*` keys dynamically from package.json, so no gate wiring changed), and every comment reference; added the missing `docs/MAP.md` row. **Deliberately NOT renamed:** the AsyncStorage key prefixes in `category-page-position.ts` / `answered-option-storage.ts` and the `full-profile:${me.id}` `storageKey` — changing those would silently wipe every user's saved page position and "Answered" stamps; also left `CategoryQuestionRow`, `uniqueCategoryAxes`, `CategoryDef` and the two lib filenames, which are still genuinely category-derived. Verified: `npm run check:ota-gate` green (typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks). Older log entries below keep the old name on purpose — they record what it was called at the time.

- 2026-09-11: **Bug fix — Sage (Q25) and Legends (Q50) were permanently unreachable for anyone who took the optional scenario phase; fixed with a count-only trait-track write.** Found by a read-only audit of the onboarding→Questions→Legends flow (same session). Root cause: the optional post-onboarding scenario phase (`OptionalIntakeSweep`, 8 screens × 2 axes = **all 16 axes**) writes with source `self_scenario`, which is **direct** (`traits.ts:48`); the frozen 50-question intake writes `self_situation`, which is **inferred** (`questions/answer.ts:57`). `shouldWriteReportTrack` (`trait-stability.ts:485`) refuses inferred-over-direct, and `collectAnswers` (`me.ts:515`) then **dropped the answer outright** — no track write, so `answerCount` never moved, so `bankTotalProgress().answered` (`questions/local.ts:203`) froze at 16 of 50, so `legendsUnlocked`/`sageUnlocked` (`questions/progressive-unlock.ts`) could never fire. The bank is **exactly 50 items**, so the gate demands a perfect score with zero slack — one stalled axis strands it. Not a refresh bug and not a nav gate: both were checked and are correct. **The trap that shaped the fix:** removing the gate would have been wrong. For report-kind answers `persistMergedTraits` copies the blended track value straight into `nextMerged.values[axis]` → `me.<axis>` (`me.ts:440-444`), so that gate — **not** `mergeTraitWrite`, whose own stickiness check at `traits.ts:184` is overwritten moments later — is the only thing protecting a direct source's stored number from inferred writes. Unblocking it would have silently rewritten users' displayed traits. **Fixed** by separating "an answer happened" from "the answer moves the number": a blocked `self_situation` answer is now emitted `countOnly: true` instead of dropped, and new `applyCountOnlyAnswer` (`trait-stability.ts`) bumps only `answerCount` + `lastTouched`, carrying `value` and `stability` through untouched, writing nothing to `me.<axis>` and appending no `trait_history` row. Narrowed to `self_situation` only — `self_game` stays excluded from report tracks entirely, `self_grid` keeps being dropped, and direct-beats-inferred is unchanged everywhere else. **Accepted side effect, acked by emci in the proposal:** `answerCount` now climbs on direct-owned axes, so at `STABILITY_FLOOR_OVERRIDE_N` (8) the inconsistent-answerer floor returns 0.05 and `isProfileSettled` reads true for an axis whose `stability` is still 0 — that un-gates Explore observations, Sage Title, Sage insight spend and the full-picture capstone on agreement-free evidence. Reachable on the three 6-item axes after one repeat pass. Left as-is because that floor exists to unstick people; the one-line alternative is to exclude count-only bumps from the override. `lastTouched` also moving means inferred answers hold off the 60-day decay clock — deliberate, documented in the docblock. **Known remaining gap, same root cause, NOT fixed (needs emci + a migration):** `claim_full_profile_complete` (`wave52`, line 183) pays the +21 ATO tokens only after **≥50 `trait_history` rows with source `self_situation`**, and `historyDiff` (`trait-history.ts:32`) emits a row only when the value actually *changes* — count-only writes change no value, so the Q50 token payout stays broken for exactly the same users. Gate: typecheck + lint + **all 74 offline checks** green, `check:progressive-unlock` 4→10 (new assertions prove the scenario-seeded path reaches 50 and unlocks both, that value/stability stay byte-identical, that the old behaviour stranded at 16, plus two source-level wiring assertions on the `me.ts` branch, since `collectAnswers`/`persistMergedTraits` are private and `me.ts` pulls in Supabase at import). One review round, PASS, no critical findings; reviewer's two actionable notes (wiring untested, decay docblock) were both taken before push. Not device-verified. No schema, auth, env, or dependency changes. Also logged from the same audit, not fixed this pass: `parse.ts:69` silently discards any AI question over 400 chars or without exactly 2–3 options (likely source of the "400 limit" report), and the 50-Q intake pager (`CategoryPagedQuestions`) and 25-Q ongoing round (`OngoingRoundFold`) are fully duplicated implementations with no shared paging code — the ongoing round has no back-navigation, no "N of 25" progress, and the intake pager has no error handling at all.

- 2026-09-10: **Bug fix — `insert_bank_pool_items` 42702 "ambiguous column" fixed via new migration `wave63`, pushed (commit `2b0c555`).** Sentry surfaced Postgres error 42702 on the ongoing-round AI-fallback path (bank comes up short for an axis). Root cause: `insert_bank_pool_items` (wave50, applied live 2026-09-09) declares `returns table(id uuid, prompt text)`, and the `prompt` OUT column becomes an implicit plpgsql variable that collides with the real `question_bank_pool.prompt` column referenced bare in the function's own `on conflict (prompt) do update set prompt = excluded.prompt` — the arbiter column reference is where plpgsql's column-ref hook actually throws (not the `set` target, which the review pass caught was a slightly-off claim in an earlier draft of the fix comment, corrected before push). Same bug class previously hit in `stage8_invite_pause_qualify_columns.sql`/`stage8_invite_referral.sql`, but those fixed it by alias-qualifying read references — not applicable here since an `on conflict` arbiter column can't be alias-qualified — so `wave63_fix_insert_bank_pool_items_ambiguous_prompt.sql` instead drops and recreates the function with the output column renamed `prompt` → `out_prompt`, removing the collision. Confirmed `create or replace function` would not have worked for this specific change (Postgres compares OUT-parameter tuple descriptors by name on `CREATE OR REPLACE`, would fail 42P13) — drop+create was required, not just a style choice. Function body otherwise byte-identical to wave50's version, including the `revoke`/`grant`/`comment on function`. Grepped every other `returns table` function in `supabase/migrations` (twice, independently, across two review passes) — none has an analogous collision; this was genuinely isolated, no downstream sweep done. No client change needed: `addToBankPool` (`src/lib/questions/bank-pool.ts:100`) only ever read `.id` off the response by array position, confirmed against both the current source and the already-shipped `dist/` bundles (so this is also compatible with builds already on devices, no forced OTA republish). Gate: typecheck clean; `check:wave50` (5/5) and `check:bank-pool` (4/4) still pass unchanged since they assert the SQL body text, not the output column name. Two review rounds, both PASS, no critical findings. **Applied live by emci shortly after this entry was first written** (confirmed via emci directly, not re-verified against the DB in this session) — `wave63` should now be in effect.

- 2026-09-10/11: **Bug fix — `ongoing-round-start` Sentry timeout (25000ms) fixed by bounding AI call latency, pushed (commit `6f6bb61`).** Surfaced right after the `wave63` fix above went live — plausible connection (not confirmed): before `wave63`, every AI-fallback draft's `insert_bank_pool_items` call threw 42702 immediately, so a round composition failed fast; once that started succeeding, the full multi-chunk AI chain could run to completion for the first time and hit its own latency ceiling instead. Root cause investigated first (`investigator`): composing a 25-item round chains up to 5 sequential `generateBatch` calls (`chunked-generate.ts`, `CHUNK_SIZE=5`), each retried up to `MAX_SHORTFALL_RETRIES` times, each of which could itself do a serial Gemini→DeepSeek fallback (`generate.ts`) — and nothing in that entire chain had its own per-call timeout, only the single outer 25s `withTimeout` wrapper around the whole composition (`questions-fold.tsx`). Any edge cold-start plus a couple of slow/retried calls trivially exceeded 25s; `docs/GOTCHAS.md:96`'s general "RN fetch has no timeout" gap, not previously connected to this specific chunk loop. **Fixed, smallest targeted change, no redesign:** (1) `ai-generate` edge function gets a new `fetchWithTimeout` (8s `AbortController`, detects via `controller.signal.aborted` not `err.name` — more robust to Deno's actual abort error shape, a fix made during the first review round) wrapping all three vendor fetch call sites; (2) client `generateText`'s edge call wrapped in a 15s `withTimeout` backstop (raised from an initial 10s during review — 10s was too tight against the edge function's own ~8-10s worst case including auth/`claim_ai_call`/cold start, and since `claim_ai_call` runs before the vendor call, a too-tight client timeout would abandon calls that had already spent a quota unit); (3) `MAX_SHORTFALL_RETRIES` lowered 2→1; (4) the outer `ongoing-round-start` timeout raised 25000ms→40000ms (`ongoing-round-load`, a separate plain read, untouched). **Known accepted gap, documented not fixed:** raising the client per-call timeout to 15s means one chunk's full retry-plus-fallback path can now take up to ~30s (two sequential 15s-backstopped calls), so a single unlucky chunk can still nearly exhaust the new 40s outer budget, and the true worst case (multiple such chunks) still exceeds it — the actual fix would be a composition-wide time budget or parallel chunk execution, a larger change out of scope here; this fix narrows the common case and gives every call a real ceiling instead of none. Lowering the retry budget also means a round can come up more often short of 25 items pre-launch, when the bank pool is still small — accepted, not silently hidden. **One real bug caught in the first review round, fixed before push:** the diff had updated the actual 25000→40000 timeout in `questions-fold.tsx` but not the matching literal in `scripts/ongoing-round-save-check.ts:138`, which would have failed `check:ongoing-round-save` and blocked `ota:publish` (the gate would have caught it before any bad OTA went out, but it was still a real miss in the first pass, not a nitpick). Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 baseline warnings unchanged) + `check:ongoing-round` (5/5) + `check:ongoing-round-save` (10/10) + `check:ai` (17/17) + `check:chunked-generate` (11/11) + full `check:ota-gate` (74/74) all green. Two review rounds, first FAIL (the stale check literal, above) then PASS. Not device-verified. No schema/auth/dependency changes; the `ai-generate` Edge Function change needs a redeploy to take effect (not just the git push) — still pending emci's go-ahead as of this entry. **Redeployed Sep 13, 2026 on emci's go-ahead** — `ai-generate` is now version 9 on project `aijzsmupaaaxjctfgwpl`; the timeout fix from commit `6f6bb61` is live.

- 2026-09-10: **Bug fix — ongoing-round "Next round" button mislabeled + generic error hid the real failure (`src/components/questions-fold.tsx`'s `OngoingRoundFold`).** Emci reported the CTA read "Next round" (should say "Submit," since it submits answers to load the next batch) and tapping it always showed "Could not load your next round. Try again." regardless of cause. Root cause investigated first: `load()` (mount-time fetch) and `start()` (button-tap compose+save) shared one boolean `error` state, so the UI rendered the identical load-failure copy no matter which action actually threw — undiagnosable from the user-facing text alone. Checked the live `ato` Supabase project directly (RPCs, RLS, bank-pool data) and found nothing obviously broken server-side, so the true underlying trigger is still unconfirmed; this fix targets the reporting gap, not a specific server bug. **Fixed:** heading renamed "Next round" → "Submit"; boolean `error` replaced with `errorKind: 'load' | 'start' | null` so each catch sets its own kind, the rendered message differs per kind ("Couldn't submit your answers." vs "Couldn't load your next round."), and the retry button now re-invokes whichever action actually failed instead of always calling `load()`. **Also added `Sentry.captureException(err)` alongside the existing `console.log` in both catch blocks** — flagged by emci that this app ships via OTA with no way to pull a real user's device console after the fact, so silent `console.log`-only failures are otherwise invisible. Matches the existing `src/lib/sentry.ts` pattern (`Sentry.init` in `_layout.tsx`, DSN-gated, `sendDefaultPii: false`); both reachable throw types (`PostgrestError`, `AiProviderError`) are real `Error` subclasses carrying no prompt/PII content. Review: PASS, no critical findings — noted but left out of scope (not requested): `pick()`/`reroll()` in the same component still only `console.log` their failures, and a now-redundant `disabled={loading || starting}` on the retry button. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 baseline warnings unchanged, none in the touched file). Not device-verified — if the underlying failure recurs, Sentry now captures it with a real stack instead of only a local console line. No schema/auth/dependency changes.

- 2026-09-10: **Three Questions screen polish fixes shipped and pushed (commit `afd6365`).** (1) Stopped dimming the just-answered option in `category-paged-questions.tsx` while the save is in flight — there was never an actual entrance animation on the "Answered" stamp; the perceived "animating in late" was the shared `busy` flag dimming the option (and its stamp) for the whole save round trip. (2) Auto-scroll to the next unanswered question after answering, same-category only (crossing category pages to reach a cross-category unanswered question was scoped out — a bigger, more disorienting move than the scroll itself; emci accepted this tradeoff during planning). Threads `intake-sweep.tsx`'s existing `ScrollView` ref down through `questions-fold.tsx` to `CategoryPagedQuestions`; per-row refs + `measureLayout` compute the target. (3) Moved the "fully unlocked" milestone toast out of the scroll flow in `intake-sweep.tsx`, pinned near the pixel avatar (`NavPixel`, global tab-shell component) so it stays visible while scrolling instead of being scrolled past unread — `MilestoneToast` gained an optional `style` override prop for this; Legends' own use of the same component is untouched. **Two real runtime bugs caught in review and fixed before push, neither would have been caught by typecheck/lint/the offline gate:** (a) the auto-scroll used `findNodeHandle()` + a numeric node handle for `measureLayout` — Fabric (the New Architecture, default since Expo SDK 54, no `newArchEnabled` override in this repo) rejects a plain number outright (dev: console.error, silent no-op; `onFail` never even fires); fixed to `ScrollView.getNativeScrollRef()`, which works on both architectures — TS allowed the broken version because `measureLayout`'s types accept `HostInstance | number`, so this was invisible to typecheck. (b) the sticky banner's position math used `top: Spacing.two` alone inside `SafeAreaView`, reasoning that `SafeAreaView` already insets its own children so adding the inset again would double-count it — wrong: an absolutely-positioned child is laid out from the containing node's border box, and `SafeAreaView` applies its inset as padding on itself, which an absolute child ignores entirely; would have rendered ~47-59pt too high, under the status bar/Dynamic Island. Fixed to `top: insets.top + Spacing.two`, matching `NavPixel`'s own positioning exactly. Also fixed in the same pass: an Android view-flattening risk (`collapsable={false}` added to the measured row, matching `NavPixel`'s own precedent for a view that must exist natively), a check-script regex false-positive (`check:category-paged-questions`'s "no nested ScrollView" assertion started matching the new `RefObject<ScrollView | null>` TYPE usage as if it were rendered JSX — narrowed to only match actual `</ScrollView>`/self-closing tags), and split `ScrollView` into a type-only import in both touched components (never rendered, only used as a type). Not device-verified (no login credentials available to this session, correctly not something to hand to an agent) — verified via typecheck, lint, `check:category-paged-questions` (5/5), `check:questions` (41/41), and the full offline gate (74/74) instead; emci should confirm the scroll-to-next-question and the avatar-banner placement look right on an actual device before relying on either.

- 2026-09-10: **Full Profile's "Answered" text label replaced with a stamp badge (commit `4184225`), pushed.** Rotated (-10°), bordered, translucent, overlaid on the selected option card in `category-paged-questions.tsx` — same `theme.textSecondary` color the old text used (no dedicated green/success token exists in this theme system). New `answered-option-storage.ts` (AsyncStorage, mirrors `category-page-position.ts`'s pattern exactly) persists which option was picked per row, since the underlying data model only ever tracked a boolean `answered`, never which option — needed so the stamp shows correctly when scrolling back to a question answered in an earlier session, not just one just tapped. **Does not backfill**: a row answered before this shipped shows no stamp (no stored index for it), an accepted tradeoff surfaced to and approved by emci before building. Review caught two real bugs before push, both fixed: (1) the stamp used to persist even when the answer write silently failed (`pickBankItem` now returns whether the save actually succeeded; the stamp is only persisted on confirmed `true`); (2) storage wasn't scoped per account — `storageKey` now includes `me.id`, closing a same-device cross-account leak (a second account would otherwise see the first account's answer stamps). New test coverage added for the storage module, including a same-tick double-save race test that specifically proves the read-modify-write fix (an earlier draft's `cache.get ?? await load` pattern could drop one of two concurrent picks). Gate: typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks green. Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified (could not sign in through the browser preview without real credentials, which is correctly not something to hand to an agent — structural/test verification only).

Emci asked to finish it rather than leave it stubbed. `rolls/compose.ts`'s legend item now computes `archetypeCode(values)` (pure) and generates a story through the same injected `generateRollText` every other roll item (category reads, story) already uses — reuses `ROLL_META`, no new AI call-site metadata, since `buildLegendStoryPrompt`'s shape (pure function of pole phrases, no name/history) matches what ROLL_META already declares. **Gated on `legendsUnlocked(tracks)`** — the same threshold the standalone Legends screen itself uses. First draft had NO gate (reasoning the old free/deterministic figure-catalog matcher always attempted a match regardless of profile depth); a review pass caught this as the wrong precedent — that old behavior was free, this is a real AI call, and Story's own gate on the very next lines of the same function exists for exactly this reason (never generate from an empty/thin profile). Two review passes: first caught the missing-gate issue (fixed), second passed clean plus two minor doc/defensive-guard nits (also fixed same session — a stale comment in `roll-history-fold.tsx`, and the shape guard above). `rolls-compose-check.ts` rewritten for the real behavior, including bumping the test fixture's `answerCount` from 5 to 20 after directly verifying (not assuming) that 5 only reached 47/50 on `bankTotalProgress` — just under `LEGENDS_UNLOCK_THRESHOLD`. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks green. `/roll` remains hidden (`HIDDEN_TAB_ROUTES`) — this only fixed its legend item, did not un-hide the tab. **This closes the entire core loop redesign plan** (`docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md`) — every T-card done. Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified (and `/roll` can't be device-tested without a manual URL/deep-link since it's hidden from nav).

- 2026-09-10: **Core loop redesign — Categorize rewrite shipped and pushed (commit `db4dc8e`): read-only AI-generated statements replace the old category_question_items Q&A entirely.** Per `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` §3 (old T-07/T-08/T-09). New `category_statements` table (wave59, applied live) + `insert_category_statements()` RPC — security definer, caps the input batch at 32 items (abuse guard, not a business-logic freeze) and validates each `category_id` against the LIVE `category_defs` table rather than a hardcoded 11-id list, since `categories.ts` deliberately keeps the catalog open-ended ("do not freeze a count"). `src/lib/category-statements/generate-statements.ts` — one AI call producing a statement for every ready category at once (batched JSON), mirrors `rolls/category-read.ts`'s prompt style; `CATEGORY_STATEMENTS_META` added to `call-sites.ts` (personalized, not bucket-shareable). Per-category archive fold (`category-statement-archive-fold.tsx`, 11 separate instances per §6, not combined — `fetchStatementHistory` called once per fold) reuses the lazy-load-on-open pattern from `roll-history-fold.tsx`/`legend-history-fold.tsx`, including the same `refreshSignal` fix (a fold opened before a regenerate would otherwise show a stale list). `categories-fold.tsx`'s old Q&A `batchList`/submit-prompt section replaced with the statement display + one batch "Generate/Regenerate statements" button (single button for all 11 at once was my own UX call, not spec'd verbatim — flagged, not silently decided). **Prompt tightened mid-build after review**: the first draft asked for 2-4 sentences per category, risking the batched JSON response exceeding `ai-generate`'s hard 1024-output-token clamp across 11 categories in one call (a truncated response fails `JSON.parse` and loses the whole batch, quota still spent) — cut to 1-2 sentences/~25 words per statement before shipping, not device-verified against a real long-profile case yet. **Old system deleted entirely, applied live in two migrations**: `wave60` drops `reroll_category_batch_item`/`answer_category_question_item`/`finalize_category_batches`/`insert_category_batch_items` and `category_question_batches` (cascade) — **found live, not assumed**: the cascade only dropped the FK *constraint* on `category_question_items`, not the table itself (real Postgres behavior: `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE` drops dependent constraints/views, never dependent tables), so a follow-up `wave61` explicitly drops `category_question_items` too. `CategoryBatchFold` deleted from `questions-fold.tsx` (`QuestionsFold`/`OngoingRoundFold` in the same file untouched, shared styles preserved, only `axisItem`/`axisItemHeader` removed); `category-batch.ts`/`category-batch-store.ts` deleted outright; old `rerollCategoryItem` removed from `reroll.ts` (`rerollQuestionItem`/`rerollLegend` untouched) — `spendAtoTokensCategoryReroll` (ato-tokens-server.ts) is left in place, currency-only, no caller until a reroll for the new statement system is built (separate, not-yet-built scope — T-10). New `src/lib/category-statements/` directory (holding `generate-statements.ts`/`store.ts`) deliberately named to avoid colliding with the existing flat `src/lib/categories.ts` module (a first pass used `src/lib/categories/`, which shadowed/collided with `categories.ts` — caught in review and renamed before push, following this codebase's existing `category-*.ts` flat-file convention). Gate: typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks green (`check:category-batch` removed, `check:reroll`/`check:ai` updated for the new call-site count of 11). Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified.

- 2026-09-10: **Legends migrations applied live — wave57 + wave58 both confirmed on the `ato` Supabase project, blocker from the previous entry closed.** Emci applied both by hand. Verified directly via SQL (not assumed): `wave58` was already fully live (`legend_generations` table, `claim_legend_story_generation`/`insert_legend_generation` RPCs, `app_config.legend_story_generations_daily_cap`), but `wave57`'s drop had NOT taken — the old `legend_figures`/`legend_variants`/`legend_archetypes`/`archetype_defs`/`user_legend_history` tables and their RPCs (`claim_legend_generation`, `insert_legend_candidate`, `approve_/reject_legend_variant`, `trait_axis_valid`) were all still live with data, and the old `app_config.legend_generations_daily_cap` column was still present. Reordered (table drops before the `trait_axis_valid` function drop) and re-applied — succeeded. Re-verified via direct SQL query afterward: zero old Legends tables/functions/columns remain, only `legend_generations` + the new RPCs/column exist. `supabase/migrations/wave57_drop_legend_candidates.sql` on disk updated to match the corrected order actually applied. **Legends is now fully live on the 64-archetype system**, ready for device testing.

(`classify.ts`/`archetypes.ts`).** Per `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` §4. **Old system deleted entirely, no data preservation (pre-launch):** tables `legend_figures`/`legend_variants`/`legend_archetypes`/`archetype_defs`/`user_legend_history` and RPCs `reject_legend_variant`/`approve_legend_variant`/`insert_legend_candidate`/`claim_legend_generation`/`trait_axis_valid` dropped in new migration `wave57_drop_legend_candidates.sql` (signatures verified against the live `create or replace function` statements before drafting the drop, not guessed — `trait_axis_valid` specifically confirmed to have zero other callers before being included); `app_config.legend_story_generations_daily_cap`, default 5/day) + `insert_legend_generation()` RPC, all in new migration `wave58_legend_generations.sql`. `src/lib/legends64/generate-story.ts` (one AI call, `LEGEND_STORY_META` — personalized, not bucket-shareable, since the plan calls for a fresh story per generation) and `store.ts` (save/fetch). `legends.tsx` fully rewired: manual "let's search your legend?" trigger (no auto-generate), 6-skin client-side switcher (`archetypeName(code, skin)`, one stored story shown under all 6 names, switching never refetches), new `rerollLegend(archetypeCode)` in `reroll.ts` (generates the story, THEN spends the existing 10-token/1-day `spendAtoTokensLegendReroll` RPC — order corrected during planning after emci caught an earlier draft's spend-before-generate mistake, verified in the actual code by the reviewer, not just asserted), new `LegendCard`/`legend-history-fold.tsx` (latest-3-visible archive, excludes the currently-shown generation, reused RollHistoryFold's lazy-load pattern rather than generalizing it). `dev-test-user.ts`'s 4 archetype presets rewritten to target specific `classify.ts` codes (e.g. `HLL-HLL`) instead of the old legend/archetype ids — `check:dev-test-user` now independently re-derives each preset's code via its own mirror of `midpointHighLow`/`archetypeCode` rather than trusting the stated field. **`/roll`'s legend item (hidden/unlaunched tab) stubbed to a permanent `{ready:false, matched:false}` placeholder rather than rewired** — explicit scope decision (emci chose "stub it out" over "rewire now" when asked, since `/roll` isn't reachable in production). **Known blocker, not yet resolved: the two new migrations are written to disk but NOT applied to the live `ato` Supabase project** — every `apply_migration` call in this session was blocked by the harness's own auto-mode permission classifier (destructive AND additive schema calls both refused, not just the drop). This is a harness permission gap, not a code defect, but it means **Legends will error for every user (missing table/RPCs) until emci applies `wave57_drop_legend_candidates.sql` then `wave58_legend_generations.sql` by hand** (Supabase SQL editor, or by granting the migration tool permission in a future session) — do this BEFORE any OTA publish touches this code path.
