- 2026-09-09: **Core loop redesign — T-11 shipped: 64-archetype classification + naming content (`src/lib/legends64/`).** First vertical slice of `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md`, no schema/deps. `classify.ts`: deterministic straight-midpoint (`>=0.5`) split on 6 axes into an 8-core x 8-modifier code, e.g. `LHH-HHH` — a NEW classification, deliberately not reusing `traitBand`'s 0.67/0.33 bands (those stay as-is for every other consumer). `archetypes.ts`: display-name content for all 6 skins (real/gaming/godType/anime/funny/dark). **Two real review rounds, not one.** First draft invented its own 96 name fragments and joined them uniformly as "descriptor + role" — worked for 2 of 6 skins (real, gaming) but produced broken/run-on names for the other 4 (`dark` skin: "The X The Y" double-articles on all 64; `funny`/`godType`/`anime`: run-on sentence-fragment joins) — caught by the reviewer, not shipped. Root cause: the invented content mixed short noun-phrases with full predicate sentences, which a flat join can't paper over. **Fix: discovered and adapted `docs/archive/LEGENDS_ARCHETYPES_DRAFT.md`** — an existing captured design doc (marked "conversational design only... not signed off as final copy") that had already worked out this exact structure with real, consistent per-skin content for all 96 entries, confirmed by cross-check to be the actual source of the plan's own worked example ("People-First Creative Director"). Adopting it wholesale fixed every broken join (uniform "descriptor + role, no articles" now reads correctly across all 384 composed names) and also corrected the build's own first-draft assumption about that example's code — it's `LHH-HHH` (core = low-conscientiousness/high-extraversion/high-openness), not the `HHH-HHH` the first draft's check script had guessed (the plan doc itself never specified a code for the example, only the display name). Since the source draft is explicitly not-signed-off, shipped behind a new `LEGENDS64_COPY_REVIEWED = false` export, same `*_COPY_REVIEWED` convention as `sage-story.ts`/`category-batch.ts` — needs emci's content read before any UI renders it as reviewed (T-13, later). One inherited quirk left deliberately unfixed, not silently patched: the anime skin's core code `LHL` and modifier code `LLH` are both literally "Senpai" in the source draft, so code `LHL-LLH` composes to "Senpai Senpai" — flagged in the file header and in this entry for emci's content pass, not rewritten unilaterally. Second review round (after the content swap) found one real remaining bug — `splitArchetypeCode` accepted a malformed 3-segment code (e.g. `HHH-HHH-XXX`) instead of rejecting it — fixed, verified with a new test. New `npm run check:legends64` (12 assertions, auto-picked-up by `check:ota-gate`) covers: code generation/uniqueness, full 384-combination name resolution, a regex scan for the double-article defect class, the corrected worked example, invalid-code/invalid-skin-returns-null-not-throw behavior, and a jargon-guard scan (via the real `matchingJargonTerm` helper, not a raw substring check) over all 96 fragments and all 384 composed names. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged, confirmed clean on all 3 new files) + `check:legends64` (12/12) both before and after both review rounds. No schema/auth/dependency/env changes — 2 new files (`src/lib/legends64/classify.ts`, `archetypes.ts`) + 1 new check script + 1 line in `package.json`. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-09: **Core loop redesign — Q8-Q11 (Claude's own follow-up questions) resolved by emci; two real gaps found and folded into the plan; still nothing built.** Reroll exclusion is permanent-forever and strictly per-user (other users still see a question one user rerolled away). Same durable exclusion regardless of whether a question came from the shared bank or was freshly AI-generated — this simplified the design: every ongoing-round question, bank-drawn or fresh, now always gets written into `question_bank_pool` before being shown to a user, so `question_items.question_bank_item_id` is never null for ongoing-round rows and `reroll.ts` needs no special case. ~100 bank size is a soft steady-state target, not a hard ceiling — it keeps growing. Old category tables/RPCs drop outright with no data-preservation step (confirmed pre-launch, no real user data to lose). **Two real gaps found during this pass, not just Q&A:** (1) `question_items.axis`'s check constraint (`wave17_infinite_questions.sql:181-187`) is missing `playfulness` — only 15 of 16 axes — a pre-existing bug the ongoing-round loop will hit immediately; fix folded into T-02. (2) The actual Q&A component to remove for Categorize, `CategoryBatchFold`, physically lives inside `src/components/questions-fold.tsx` (shared with the unrelated `QuestionsFold`/Infinite Questions component), not in `categories-fold.tsx` as earlier plan text implied — `categories-fold.tsx` only imports it. Checked `milestones.ts` for any category-batch-completion dependency before confirming the drop is safe — none found. Full detail in `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md`. Still zero code/schema changes — planning only. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-09: **Core loop redesign — all 7 open questions from `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` §7 resolved by emci; plan doc updated in place, still nothing built.** Key decisions now load-bearing on the plan: `/roll`'s legend item stays unwired/broken (hidden tab, acceptable); Legend skin-switching shows the SAME generated story under all 6 titles (story doesn't vary by skin); Categorize archive is 11 separate per-category folds, each with its own reroll button, not combined; question bank pool seeds from the existing 50-question `QUESTIONS_BANK` and grows toward ~100 via AI top-up, staying evenly distributed per the existing tiered axis allocation; intake skip (already shipped, axis-level, `deferral.ts`) and post-50 reroll (net-new, question-instance-level, paid, **permanent** per-user exclusion via a new `question_bank_reroll_exclusions` table) are explicitly NOT the same mechanic — no reroll on the intake, ever; Categorize's old RPCs (`answer_category_question_item`, `finalize_category_batches`) AND tables (`category_question_items`, `category_question_batches`) get dropped entirely, not left dormant, sequenced strictly after the new statement path is proven live. Also added session-boundary guidance to the build order (§8): every T-card is a clean breakpoint, run each as its own fresh session by default — none of them leave mid-edit work for the next card to continue. Claude flagged 4 of its own open questions in §7 that emci hasn't answered yet (not guessed at): whether the reroll exclusion is truly permanent-forever vs. time-bounded; whether an AI-generated (non-bank) rerolled-away question needs the same durable exclusion guarantee as a bank one; whether the ~100 bank-pool target is a hard ceiling or a soft steady-state; and whether dropping the old category tables should preserve any in-progress category-batch answers first. Full detail in `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md`. Still zero code/schema changes — planning only. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-09: **Core loop redesign — mechanical implementation plan written, `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` (planning only, nothing built, nothing pushed to schema).** Emci finalized a 6-item product plan for the core loop (unlock gating, questions, categorize, legends, tokens, archive); investigated current code (via `investigator`) before writing anything down, per the standing rule. **Real corrections to the finalized plan, not just a restate of it:** (1) item 1 (Sage/Legends unlock at Q25/Q50, plain answer-count gate) is **already shipped** — `src/lib/questions/progressive-unlock.ts`'s `sageUnlocked`/`legendsUnlocked` already do exactly this, order-independent, locked-banner-not-hidden already the pattern on both screens; nothing to build there. (2) item 3 (Categorize) already has no dedicated tab — category batches already render inside Explore via `categories-fold.tsx`; the real work is converting its Q&A flow to read-only AI statements, not moving screens. (3) item 4 (Legends) is a much bigger replacement than "swap the matcher" — the live system is a DB-backed pre-authored historical-figure content catalog (`legend_figures`/`legend_variants`/`legend_archetypes`/`archetype_defs`, waves 25/26/28/32/33), not a static 12-archetype file; replacing it with the 64-archetype fresh-AI-story system also breaks `src/lib/rolls/compose.ts`'s roll-legend-item generation (`buildLegendView` call), which the finalized plan never mentions — flagged as an open question (§7 in the plan doc), not decided unilaterally; recommendation is to leave `/roll` (a hidden, unlaunched tab) broken/unwired there for now. (4) item 2's "question bank" needs genuinely new shared/DB-backed infrastructure (`question_bank_pool`) — it is not a reframing of the existing frozen 50-question `QUESTIONS_BANK` static file, which stays as-is for the frozen intake. (5) `fetchRecentTexts` (the dormant `ongoing-round.ts`'s uncalled dep) has no existing implementation anywhere to "wire up" — recommended real data source is `question_items` (wave17, already shaped for per-user tagged-axis rounds via `question_packs`), not `category_question_items` (being removed per item 3). Full mechanical plan — schema/table/RPC/file-level detail, a 15-card build order with dependencies, and 7 open questions for emci (roll's legend item, skin-vs-story coupling, category archive layout, bank seeding, pre-50 question reroll scope, whether to drop or just stop-using the removed category RPCs/tables, and the exact token-balance column name) — is in `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md`. Nothing here touches schema, auth, deps, or env — planning only, no code changes. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Bug fix — Questions screen options gave no visual confirmation of which one was tapped, reported as "tapping seems to answer a different question."** Investigated first: traced all three question-answering surfaces (Infinite Questions in `questions-fold.tsx`'s `QuestionsFold`, category-batch mode in the same file's `CategoryBatchFold`, and Full Profile's `CategoryPagedQuestions`) and found no index/order-drift bug at all — every tap handler already closes over the actual question/option object (or, for category-batch, the persisted `answeredOption` index), never a shared/stale index that could point at the wrong row. The real bug was the report's second half: no surface highlighted the tapped option, so a correct answer with zero visual feedback read as "it answered the wrong question." Fixed by adding a highlight (reusing `theme.backgroundSelected`, the same token `appearance-picker.tsx` already uses for this) to the tapped option in all three surfaces, plus `accessibilityState={{selected}}` so VoiceOver gets the same confirmation (added in the review-fix pass, was missing from the first draft). Infinite Questions also clears its highlight if the save actually throws (also a review-fix — the first draft set the highlight optimistically before the async save, so a failed save left a stale option looking picked, which is exactly what a save failure should never look like). Full Profile's fix is intentionally session-local (`pickedByRow`, not persisted) since Full Profile rows are deliberately re-answerable — a design choice already documented in that file — so this only needed to confirm the tap just made, not restore historical selections after a remount. Category-batch's fix was purely additive: it already tracked `answeredOption` per row for its existing disable-after-answer behavior, just never used it for styling. Gate: typecheck + lint (clean on both touched files) + `check:questions` (41/41) + `check:category-paged-questions` (4/4) + full `check:ota-gate` (66/66) all green, run before and after the review-fix pass. No schema/auth/save-path/dependency changes — pure UI state in `src/components/questions-fold.tsx` and `src/components/category-paged-questions.tsx`. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — §12 build order finished: items 8 (history/archive views) and 9 (Story's move to Home + a real locked state) shipped together, one review round.** Explicit budget constraint this slice (emci running low on credits) — worked leaner than prior slices: no dedicated `investigator` delegation (read the plan's §8/§9 sections and the relevant existing files directly instead), one combined reviewer pass covering both items instead of two, built both in the same pass. **§8:** new `fetchRevealedRollItems(userId, types)` in `src/lib/rolls/store.ts` — every past-revealed `trait_rolls` row of the given type(s), across ALL rolls (not just the latest, which is all `fetchRollItems`/`fetchLatestRollId` from the item-7 slice could reach), RLS-scoped, capped at 50 rows, newest reveal first. New `RollHistoryFold` component (`src/components/roll-history-fold.tsx`) — a collapsed-by-default fold (reusing `SettingsFold`, the same pattern every other Explore section already uses) that loads lazily on first open, not on mount, since most visits never open it. Wired into Explore (legend + category history, replacing where Story used to render) and Home (Story history, alongside the moved current-Story fold). Extracted the shared item-rendering logic (`RollItemBody`, `itemTitle`, `categoryLabel`) out of `roll.tsx` into `src/components/roll-item-body.tsx` so the Roll screen and the new history folds share one implementation instead of duplicating it. **§9:** `SageStoryFold` moved from Explore to Home, directly below the daily check-in card (per the pre-approved placement from an earlier slice's audit) and before "This week"/`CategoryTeaser`. Added a real locked state for the genuinely-not-ready case — previously the component rendered nothing at all whenever `!story?.body`, with no way to distinguish "profile not ready" from "still generating" or "quota refused"; now `tracksReady && !crisisToday && !storyReady(tracks)` renders the same `PROFILE_LOCKED_COPY`/`PROFILE_LOCKED_CTA` pattern Legends already uses (CTA to `/intake-sweep` targeting `missingAxis`), while every other silent case is untouched, matching the plan's actual stated scope (only the "not ready" case needed real UI). Home gained a `bootstrapReady` flag (Home's tracks come from one `fetchHomeBootstrap` call, not the separate-fetch pattern Explore/Legends use, so there was no existing readiness signal) to stop the locked state flashing before the first bootstrap resolves. **One review round found and fixed 4 real issues, not 0:** (1) a genuine crash path — `RollHistoryFold` rendered `RollItemBody` for every revealed row with no readiness gate (`roll.tsx` gates on `rollItemResultIsReady`, the history fold didn't), so a legend row revealed before wave47's ready-check existed (a dev-DB-only path today, but a real one) would destructure `variant.name` off `undefined` and crash; fixed by filtering to ready rows before rendering. (2) the history fold's error state was terminal — closing and reopening the fold couldn't retry a transient failure, only a full screen remount could; added a retry button. (3) `bootstrapReady` was never reset on a user change (e.g. dev-test-user preset switching accounts), so one render could show locked/ready computed from the previous user's tracks; fixed with a small effect keyed on `userId` alone — deliberately NOT reset on every `reloadHome` call (which also fires on every logged check via `onChecksChanged`), since that would flash the Story lock on ordinary check-ins. (4) `sage-story-fold.tsx`'s docstring still said "under pinned Categories on Explore," stale after the move; corrected. Updated `scripts/wave22-check.ts` and `scripts/explore-check.ts`, which both previously asserted Story lived on Explore, to assert the new Home placement and Explore's non-reference instead — confirmed as honest updates (testing the new real behavior), not weakened. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, baseline warnings unchanged) + `check:rolls-store` extended to 11/11 (new `fetchRevealedRollItems` assertions) + `check:wave22` (8/8, updated) + `check:explore` (35/35, updated) + full `check:ota-gate` (66/66) all green. No schema/migration/auth/dependency changes — `fetchRevealedRollItems` is a read against the already-applied, already-RLS-protected `trait_rolls` table. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — §12 build-order item 7 completed: the roll/reveal mechanic now has a real screen, `src/app/(tabs)/roll.tsx`.** Everything below it (compose/store/run) was already shipped and reviewed in the sixth/seventh slices; this is the first UI consumer. Reached via a "Roll" CTA on Legends (`legends.tsx`), shown only once `legendsUnlocked` (gated on `tracksReady` too, not just `!locked`, since `locked` reads false both when genuinely unlocked and mid-load — a real flash bug caught in review). The screen shows the token balance, a Roll CTA when `rollEligible()` says yes, and — once a roll exists — all 13 items as cards: unrevealed-but-ready ones show a price and Reveal button (5 tokens for legend, 1 for category/story, mirroring `reveal_roll_item`'s own hardcoded SQL pricing via a new **display-only** `rollItemPrice()` in `compose.ts` — the RPC's own returned `price` stays authoritative), not-ready ones show "still forming," revealed ones show their body. **Two real gaps found and fixed via two review rounds, not one:** (1) **Revealed content was unrecoverable after a reload.** `runRoll()` only ever returns a `roll_id` string — nothing previously read a stored roll's 13 rows back for display, so a user who spent tokens revealing an item, then left the screen or reloaded, had no path back to it (`fetchLastRollSnapshot` returns only the axis snapshot, not items). Fixed by adding `fetchRollItems(rollId)` and `fetchLatestRollId(userId)` to `store.ts` (both plain reads against `trait_rolls`, scoped by the table's pre-existing `trait_rolls_select_own` RLS policy — no new migration) and a mount-once effect (guarded by a ref keyed on `me.id`) that restores the latest roll's items on load instead of only reacting to a fresh `runRoll()` call in the same session. (2) **A combined eligibility+display effect could silently wipe a displayed roll.** The first draft used one effect that fetched the snapshot and set the whole screen's `state` to `{status:'idle', eligible}` on every `tracks` change — since `state` was also what held displayed roll items, any tracks refetch while items were on screen (e.g. after `refresh()` post-reveal) risked clobbering them back to an idle/error screen. Fixed by splitting eligibility into its own `eligible`/`eligibleReady` state, updated by a separate effect that never touches `state` — only the mount-once loader (and `onRoll`) can set `state` to `'items'`. A second reviewer pass caught a residual version of the same class of bug (`eligible` had no loaded/error state — a first-ever user could see "nothing new to roll" flash before the real eligibility check returned, or get stuck there forever if the snapshot fetch failed) — fixed by adding `eligibleReady`, gating the whole eligible/not-eligible render branch on it. Other review-fix-round findings: an unreachable "no legend matched" fallback branch removed (a `ready:true` legend item is always paired with `matched:true` + `variant` + `archetype` per `compose.ts`'s own item-building code — verified before removing, not assumed); the `!CATEGORY_READ_COPY_REVIEWED`/`!STORY_COPY_REVIEWED` draft-copy badge (a CLAUDE.md hard invariant — Story/category-read text is diagnosis-adjacent and unreviewed) was missing entirely from the first draft, added matching `sage-story-fold.tsx`'s exact pattern, gated to category/story reveals only (legend copy already ships in production); `onReveal`'s local-state update switched to a functional `setState` updater to close a reveal-race window flagged in review. Gate: typecheck + lint (clean on every touched file) + `check:rolls-store` extended to 10/10 (new assertions for `fetchRollItems`/`fetchLatestRollId`/`rollItemPrice`) + full `check:ota-gate` (66/66) all green, run through both review-fix rounds. No schema/migration/auth/dependency changes — `fetchRollItems`/`fetchLatestRollId` are reads against an already-applied, already-RLS-protected table. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — seventh slice: roll RPC wiring (§7) shipped, wave48 migration created (NOT yet applied).** New `src/lib/rolls/results.ts` (pure JSON parsing, split out so it stays Node-testable — store.ts imports `@/lib/supabase`, which pulls in react-native), `store.ts` (the 4 real RPC callers: `claimRoll`, `claimRollGeneration`, `fetchLastRollSnapshot`, `storeRoll`, `revealRollItem`), `generate.ts` (the one real `generateText` call site for rolls, new `ROLL_META` declared in `src/lib/ai/call-sites.ts`), and `run.ts` (`runRoll` — the actual top-level entry point tying eligibility, quota, composition, and storage together). Not wired into any screen yet. Four review rounds, found and fixed two genuine architecture gaps plus two resulting bugs — not style nitpicks: **(1) Quota isolation was completely disconnected from reality.** Traced the actual dispatch code and found `src/lib/ai/edge.ts` never sends a `callType` to `ai-generate` at all — every AI feature in this app, not just rolls, claims against the shared Sage/Explore pool (`claim_ai_call`, defaults to `'sage'`) regardless of its own declared metadata. This meant a roll's ~12 generations would burn ~12 of a user's 20 daily Sage/Explore calls, with `claim_roll` (wave46) never actually consulted by the generation path. Fixed with a new `claim_roll_generation()` RPC (wave48) that mirrors the proven, already-live `claim_questions_batch` (wave17) exactly — its own `ai_usage.by_type` key, own `app_config.roll_generations_daily_cap` (default 15), claimed once per generation attempt *before* `generateText` runs. Deliberately did NOT modify the already-live `ai-generate` Edge Function itself — a much larger, riskier change with no way to test it live (this Supabase project has no branching on its current plan) — so generations still also claim the shared pool afterward too, same as every other feature already does today; this is not a regression rolls introduced, and the new dedicated cap is what actually delivers §7's stated intent of bounding a roll's own cost. **(2) RCI eligibility runs client-side only, not server-enforced** as §7 literally specifies ("must run inside the RPC itself, never trusted from the client"). Assessed and accepted as a known, documented gap rather than built speculatively into a real Edge Function (same reasoning as above — can't test Edge Function changes live) — the actual AI-cost exposure this could open is still hard-bounded by two independent, real server-side RPCs regardless of whether eligibility was honestly evaluated (`claimRoll`, 1 composition/day; `claimRollGeneration`, ~15 generations/day), matching the same trust model this repo already accepts for other bounded-but-unverified actions like `claim_intake_complete`. Both `run.ts`'s and `compose.ts`'s docstrings were corrected to state this honestly — an earlier draft of both falsely claimed the requirement was satisfied by a nonexistent Edge Function; the same false claim also existed in wave46's own SQL comments (both the function's and a column's), corrected via a new `comment on` statement in wave48 since wave46 is already applied live and can't be edited retroactively. **Resulting bug found on a later pass:** `claimRollGeneration` throws on any RPC error (by design, matching `claimRoll`'s shape) — but `composeRoll`'s `generateRollText` dependency contract requires "never throws, degrades to null on any failure." An uncaught throw there would have aborted the entire roll from a single transient failure, after `claimRoll` had already spent the day's one composition attempt. Fixed at two layers: `generateRollItemText` now wraps its own claim+generate calls in try/catch, and `runRoll` wraps its entire body too as a backstop — verified end-to-end (with wave48 still unapplied today) that a `claim_roll_generation`-not-found error now degrades every item to `{ready:false}` and still returns a clean `{kind:'stored'}` result rather than crashing. Also fixed: the roll's legend item was storing the full `LegendMatch` object (including the complete story body) — trimmed to just what a roll actually needs to display, since the full story is already reachable through the existing Legends feature. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + new `check:wave48` (4/4), updated `check:rolls-compose` (9/9) and `check:rolls-store` (7/7) + full `check:ota-gate` (66/66) all green, run through all four review-fix rounds. Schema file: `supabase/migrations/wave48_roll_generation_quota.sql` — needs emci's review/apply, alongside wave46/47. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — sixth slice: roll composition orchestration (§7), wave45+wave46 confirmed applied live by emci; wave47 created (NOT yet applied) to patch a real gap wave46 shipped with.** New `src/lib/rolls/compose.ts` (`composeRoll`, `rollEligible`) and `src/lib/rolls/category-read.ts` (the new per-category narrative prompt, mirroring `buildStoryPrompt`'s pattern but scoped to one category and — the one deliberate difference from Story — allowed to name it). Pure orchestration, dependency-injected (no Supabase/ai-generate import), same pattern `composeCategoryBatch`/`fillAxisCountsChunked` already use; assembles the exact 13-item payload (1 legend + 11 categories + 1 story) `store_roll` (wave46) hard-validates. Not wired into any live route/Edge Function yet — infra-first, same scoping every other slice has used. One review round, real findings on both the TS and (retroactively) the SQL side: **(1) a tautological guard.** The first version's category-count check compared `getCategoryDefs().length` against itself via the same synchronous call — could never fire, even though the live `category_defs` table genuinely can drift (it's grown twice before, wave21 and wave40) or contain duplicates. Fixed with a literal `ROLL_CATEGORY_COUNT = 11` (matching store_roll's own hard-coded requirement) plus a real distinct-id check, and a test that calls the actual `setCategoryDefs()` with a wrong-count list and confirms `composeRoll` throws — proving the guard now genuinely fires. Flagged as a known, accepted coupling: if the category catalog ever grows again, both this constant and store_roll's schema need updating together — not a new risk this introduced, since store_roll's own hard-13 requirement already baked the same assumption in. **(2) A content-safety slip.** The category-read prompt's own RULES text named a real internal axis label ("never say 'conscientiousness'") as a "don't say this" example — which puts that exact term into the prompt sent to the model, directly contradicting framework-fence's own stated principle ("do not put this term list into the model prompt — listing them teaches the tokens"). Fixed by keeping the rule generic instead of giving a concrete example. **(3) Inconsistent result shapes and a resulting real "pay for nothing" gap.** Legend items used `{matched: boolean}`, category/story used `{ready: boolean, body?}` — no single field a caller could check uniformly. Unified: every item now also carries a top-level `ready: boolean` (legend: `ready: matched`). This unification is what made a real, already-live bug fixable: `reveal_roll_item` (wave46, confirmed applied to production) never checked whether an item actually had anything to reveal — a user could spend a token on a category whose generation had failed, or a legend that matched nothing, and get empty content for their token. Since wave46 is already live, fixed via a NEW migration (`wave47_reveal_requires_ready.sql`, redefines just that one function, same signature) rather than editing the applied one — refuses to charge (`{ok:false, reason:'not_ready'}`) unless `result->>'ready'` is true, checked before any pricing/balance/spend logic runs. **(4) Story generation ignored its own readiness gate.** `composeRoll` always generated a story regardless of profile completeness, spending an AI call to ask for diagnosis-adjacent prose from an empty settled-notes list on a thin profile. Fixed to check `storyReady(tracks, now)` first, matching the existing Story surface's own gate (`sage-story.ts`) — skips generation and stores `{ready:false}` the same way an unready category already did. Threaded `now` through the whole composer so every readiness check and the stored snapshot use the same timestamp, not several implicit `new Date()` calls. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + `check:rolls-compose` (8/8) + new `check:wave47` (4/4) + full `check:ota-gate` (64/64) all green. Only new schema file: `supabase/migrations/wave47_reveal_requires_ready.sql` — needs emci's review/apply, same as wave45/46 were. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — fifth slice: the roll/reveal mechanic schema (§7), wave46 migration created (NOT applied to live DB yet — needs emci's review/apply).** Adds `claim_roll()` (own AI-quota call_type, own `app_config.rolls_daily_cap` default 1/day, mirrors `claim_questions_batch` exactly rather than sharing Sage/Explore's pool — a single roll is ~12 AI generations), `trait_rolls` (one row per item, 13/roll: 1 legend + 11 categories + 1 story), `trait_roll_snapshots` (one row per roll — not per axis — holding every answered axis's value+stability for RCI to compare a future roll against), `store_roll()` (atomic write of a roll's items + snapshot), and `reveal_roll_item()` (atomic spend+reveal). **Three review rounds, five real bugs found and fixed, all before anything touched a live database:** (1) `store_roll` originally trusted the caller's item array completely — no count bound, no per-item validation — the same bug *class* as wave45's unbounded mint, on writes instead of value; fixed with hard validation (exactly 13 items, exactly 1 legend/1 story/11 distinct categories via a `count(*) filter` query, per-item type/category_id/result-shape checks, an 8KB per-item size cap) plus two new partial unique indexes on `trait_rolls` as defense-in-depth (a plain `unique(roll_id, type, category_id)` wouldn't stop two legend rows, since `category_id` is NULL for both and NULLs are distinct from each other in Postgres — needed two separate partial indexes). (2) `reveal_roll_item` used a differently-suffixed advisory lock key than every other token-mutating RPC, so it wouldn't have mutually excluded with a concurrent `earn_tokens`/`spend_tokens` call for the same user; fixed to share the exact same lock key. (3) A roll_id collision between two different users would have silently left the second one with no RCI snapshot ever stored (`ok:true`, no error) instead of failing loudly; fixed to raise an exception. (4) **The most subtle one, found on a second review pass specifically because static review alone can't execute PL/pgSQL:** a local variable named `item` collided with an `AS item` query alias under Postgres's default `variable_conflict = error` — `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` only checks syntax at apply time, not this, so the migration would have applied clean and then failed with "column reference is ambiguous" on every real call to `store_roll`. Fixed by renaming the variable and dropping the alias (using the implicit `value` column name, matching this repo's own existing convention elsewhere). (5) The result-presence check (`value->'result' is null`) only caught a missing key (SQL NULL), not a caller-supplied JSON `null` literal, which would have passed and still satisfied the column's `NOT NULL` constraint; fixed to reject both explicitly. **Attempted to close the remaining verification gap for real** (nothing here has actually executed against a live Postgres) by creating a disposable Supabase dev branch to apply and test both migrations — asked emci first since it's a real, if trivial ($0.01344/hr), cost against their account; branching turned out to require a Pro-plan-or-above Supabase plan this project doesn't have, so that path isn't available. Shipped on the strength of the static review alone (3 rounds, both migrations) rather than force a live test. **Story's reveal price (1 token) is an assumption, not from the plan** — §7 only prices legend (5) and category (1) explicitly; disclosed in the migration's own comment, needs emci's explicit yes before apply, not just a silent default. `claim_roll` itself has a known, accepted gap: nothing stops an authenticated client from calling it directly (up to the daily cap) with no roll ever actually generated — assessed in review as an acceptable, self-DoS-only gap for schema-only groundwork (mints no tokens, generates nothing, burns only the caller's own counter); the real eligibility gate (RCI) is meant to run in whichever Edge Function orchestrates a roll, not yet built. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + new `check:wave46` (7/7) + full `check:ota-gate` (62/62) all green, run through all three review-fix rounds. Only schema file touched: `supabase/migrations/wave46_trait_rolls.sql` — not yet applied. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — fourth slice: token economy (§7), wave45 migration created (NOT applied to live DB yet — needs emci's review/apply, same as every prior schema-only commit).** First real schema piece of the redesign. Scope trimmed live during review from what was originally drafted: shipped `intake_complete` (+20 tokens, once ever per user, new `claim_intake_complete()` RPC); **`round_complete` (+13/round) is schema-only** — the `round_number` column, its `round_complete` reason value, and its own unique index are laid down as forward-compatible groundwork, but its earn RPC is deliberately withheld until real round-tracking state exists to validate a client-supplied round number against (none exists anywhere in the schema yet — that lands with the ongoing post-intake round persistence, not yet built). Two rounds of review, two real critical bugs found and fixed, both before anything was applied to a live database: **(1) a rescope that would have broken a live, working feature.** Scoping the pre-existing `token_events_earn_once_per_day` index to exclude the two new reasons (needed — left unscoped it would have silently capped "once ever"/"once per round" at "once per day" too) breaks Postgres's ON CONFLICT arbiter inference for the pre-existing `earn_tokens` RPC, since its `ON CONFLICT ... WHERE delta > 0` clause no longer matches (Postgres requires the ON CONFLICT predicate to imply the index's actual predicate) — every `check_in`/`game_round` earn would have errored at runtime the moment this migration was applied. Fixed by redefining `earn_tokens` inside this same migration with a matching ON CONFLICT predicate; a second review pass diffed it mechanically against the original and confirmed zero other drift. **(2) An unbounded token-mint exploit.** The originally-drafted `claim_round_complete(p_round_number)` trusted a client-supplied round number completely, with no real server-side state anywhere to validate it against — callable with an ever-incrementing number, forever, 13 free tokens each time. Not consistent with this codebase's actual trust pattern (every other trust-the-caller earn RPC, e.g. `earn_tokens('check_in')`, is hard-bounded by a real-world constraint like a calendar day; this had none). Fixed by removing the RPC entirely from this migration rather than half-fixing it, deferring it to when real round state exists. Also closed proactively (not left for later): a CHECK constraint tying `round_number IS NOT NULL` to `reason = 'round_complete'`, since NULLs are distinct in a unique index and would otherwise silently bypass the dedupe; all `drop`/`create index` statements made idempotent (`IF EXISTS`/`IF NOT EXISTS`), matching this repo's convention, since the original wasn't safely re-runnable. Client-side: `src/lib/tokens.ts`/`tokens-server.ts` gained `TOKEN_EARN_INTAKE_COMPLETE`/`claimIntakeComplete()` only — no `claimRoundComplete` wrapper exists to dangle against a nonexistent RPC. `claimIntakeComplete` has no caller yet (not wired into any UI — that's the next slice, once the frozen-intake completion flow triggers it). Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + new `check:wave45` (14/14, source-assertion-only since nothing here runs against a live DB offline) + full `check:ota-gate` (61/61) all green, run through both review-fix rounds. Only schema file touched: `supabase/migrations/wave45_trait_redesign_tokens.sql` — not yet applied. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — third slice: RCI re-roll trigger (§5) shipped, `src/lib/rci.ts`.** Pure logic only, no schema/UI yet — no caller until §7's roll mechanic (next slice) exists to consume it. Before building, ran a full audit of everything left in the plan (§5/§7/§8/§9) against current code in one pass, per emci's ask to front-load every remaining decision rather than being interrupted repeatedly; that audit's findings and the resulting pre-approvals (RCI's `sqrt(priorVariance)` formula, a new `'roll'` AI quota call_type, top-ranked-candidate legend selection, Story's Home placement, plus draft SQL for 3 upcoming schema pieces) are recorded in this session's transcript, to be spent as each of those slices is actually built. **The implementation went through two real calibration rounds, not one:** RCI's `standard_error` derives from `1 - effectiveStability` the same way T-01's Kalman `prior_variance` does, but needs its OWN floor constant (`RCI_MIN_VARIANCE`), deliberately NOT a reuse of `KALMAN_MIN_VARIANCE` (0.05) — the two protect against different failures (one guarantees a trait value can never get permanently stuck; RCI only needs to tell real change apart from noise), and reusing 0.05 caps RCI at ~1.7 for the plan's own §5 worked example (a sustained axis flip), a hair over the 1.65 cutoff instead of the plan's described unambiguous ~9.5. First attempt picked `RCI_MIN_VARIANCE = 0.001` without solving for both of the plan's worked examples at once — reviewer caught that it satisfied only the "should trigger" example, and a dynamic `applyEwmaAnswer` simulation of the "should NOT trigger" example (a drift that genuinely re-settles, not just two hand-picked stability snapshots) crossed the cutoff once stability climbed high enough for that floor to dominate. Fixed by solving both examples' own written numbers simultaneously for the one floor value that satisfies both: `RCI_MIN_VARIANCE = 0.0016` (worked example 1's drift converges to a proven, empirically-verified asymptotic RCI of exactly 1.5, a real ~0.15 margin under the cutoff, not a knife-edge; worked example 2 converges to exactly the plan's own "~9.5"). This was treated as a bug-fix-level correction (a value uniquely determined by reconciling the plan's own two worked examples), not a fresh product decision requiring another round-trip — same class of call as T-01's `KALMAN_MEASUREMENT_NOISE` calibration. Reviewer's other findings, addressed: `hasReliableChange` never reads the `game` track (same rule as everywhere else, now covered by a dedicated test); the stored snapshot's `stability` field is captured per §5's spec but deliberately unused by the RCI formula itself (documented, not a bug — the plan is explicit that standard_error derives from CURRENT stability, not the snapshot's); decay reaching RCI through `effectiveStability` (read-side only) means an axis idle long enough to decay, then answered once, can jump from below the cutoff to above it without the trait value itself moving much — accepted as consistent with how confidence already decays everywhere else in this app (Legends, Sage Title, Explore), not special-cased. Test coverage: 12 assertions including a negative-RCI (decrease) case, the exact 1.65 boundary (strict `>`, not `>=`), a decayed-vs-fresh same-raw-stability contrast, and the dynamic full-trajectory simulation for worked example 1 (not just static stability snapshots, which is exactly what let the first floor guess through undetected). Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + new `check:rci` (12/12) + full `check:ota-gate` (60/60) all green, run before and after both the reviewer-fix pass and the subsequent calibration fix. No schema/auth/dependency/env changes. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — second slice (T-05 through T-09) shipped: frozen 50-question intake, progressive unlock (Sage at Q25, Legends at Q50).** Continuation of the first slice above, on the same plan (`docs/archive/TRAIT_SYSTEM_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` §3/§6). Before building, ran a full audit of the REMAINING phases (§5 RCI, §7 tokens/rolls/reveals, §8 history views, §9 Story move) against current code to front-load every open decision in one pass (per emci's explicit ask) rather than stopping repeatedly — found and got explicit sign-off on: RCI's `standard_error = sqrt(priorVariance)` (calibrated against the existing Kalman `priorVariance` transform, not a new field); a new `'roll'` AI quota `call_type` (a single roll needs ~12 generations — reusing the existing `claim_ai_call` as-is would let one roll exhaust a user's whole daily cap); picking the top-ranked `buildLegendView` candidate as a roll's stored legend result; Story's Home placement (directly below the daily check-in card, above `CategoryTeaser`). Also pre-approved (not yet written) exact SQL for 3 schema pieces the next phases need: `token_events` gets a `round_number` column + 2 new reason values + reworked unique indexes (a real conflict was found and fixed in the draft: the existing `token_events_earn_once_per_day` index is an unconditional once-per-day rule that would have silently capped the new "once ever"/"once per round" reasons too), plus two new tables (`trait_rolls`, `trait_roll_snapshots`). **T-05** (`src/lib/questions/bank.ts`): the 48-question flat 3-per-axis bank replaced with a hand-authored 50-question set matching the §2 tiered per-axis counts (tier1/2 axes: 6 each, tier3: 4 each, tier4×10: 2 each) — hand-authored rather than live-generated (AI-assisted authoring, same one-time-content convention as the archetype names), all 12 new questions individually checked against the real guard word/phrase lists before writing, not just left to the check suite to catch. New `check:frozen-intake` asserts axis coverage, required per the plan ("must fail if a 17th axis is ever added without regenerating"). **T-06:** confirmed no hardcoded "48" existed anywhere outside comments (fixed the comments; no real logic depended on the literal number). **T-07** (`src/lib/questions/progressive-unlock.ts`, `sage.tsx`): Sage — previously fully unlocked, no gate at all — now locks below 25 answered, using the same live-derived (never stored) pattern `isProfileSettled` already used for Legends. **T-08** (`legends.tsx`, `milestones.ts`): Legends' gate retargeted from `isProfileSettled` to the new `legendsUnlocked` (answered >= 50), per emci's explicit call — the tiered intake alone never satisfies `isProfileSettled` for 10 of 16 axes (they only reach 2 intake answers, below the existing 3-answer stability floor), so keeping that gate would have kept Legends locked past question 50 for most users; the existing "Legends unlocked!" celebration was retargeted to the same threshold so the toast and the visual unlock always fire together. **T-09** (`milestones.ts`): two new milestone defs, `sage_unlocked` (25) and `profile_fully_unlocked` (50, distinct banner alongside the retargeted `legends_unlocked`) — both auto-wire through the existing `crossedMilestonesFor`/`bankTotalProgress` central mechanism with zero new plumbing. Reviewer found one **critical** bug before ship: Sage's `locked` gate was render-only — during the tracks-loading window (`tracksReady` still false) it evaluates `false` same as genuinely-unlocked, and `send()` had no independent guard, so a locked user tapping a suggestion chip in that window would get a real, paid Sage reply; fixed by adding an explicit `locked` check inside `send()` itself, not relying on the composer failing to render (this is exactly why Legends' identical-looking gate was safe and Sage's wasn't — Legends' matching is free/static, Sage's isn't). Reviewer also caught a real bank-ordering bug: one new `conflict_assertiveness` question was *inserted* mid-group instead of appended, which would have silently reordered an existing user's already-answered position and both skipped the genuinely-new question and re-surfaced an old one as "current" — fixed, and verified via a prefix-diff script that all 16 axis groups are now pure append (tier1/2/3) or pure trim-from-the-end (tier4), matching every axis's original answered order exactly. Also fixed: `profile_fully_unlocked`'s copy claimed "nothing left to unlock," which is false — Explore observations, Sage Title, Sage insight, and the full-picture capstone all still gate on `isProfileSettled` separately, unreachable by the intake alone for 10 axes; softened to not overclaim. **Known, accepted gaps flagged but not fixed this slice** (documented here, not silently swept): an existing user who fully answered the OLD 48-question bank now computes to 38/50 — Legends re-locks and Sage locks for the first time for them; the plan has no migration/grandfather story for this, worth a product call before this reaches real users. At Q50, three milestones (`answers_50`/`legends_unlocked`/`profile_fully_unlocked`) cross in the same pass and intake-sweep.tsx's central mechanism persists first, so legends.tsx's own on-tab celebration effect will now rarely be the one that actually fires (first-to-persist wins, same accepted co-crossing pattern this codebase already uses elsewhere for `bankTotalProgress` milestones) — the Legends-unlock toast will typically appear on the Questions screen now, not the Legends tab. §6's literal spec says Sage/Legends should be "hidden" pre-unlock; both ship as a visible locked card instead, matching Legends' own pre-existing convention (never hidden) rather than introducing tab-hiding as new scope. **Could not visually verify in a browser** — this app is invite-only and the dev-test auto-login was intentionally removed 2026-09-03; reviewer confirmed the JSX/logic is statically sound and typecheck-clean, but nobody has seen these two screens actually render. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, 55 warnings baseline unchanged) + 2 new checks (`check:frozen-intake` 2/2, `check:progressive-unlock` 4/4) + 4 existing checks updated for the new bank shape (`check:questions`, `check:wave19`, `check:milestones`, `check:category-paged-questions`, all now deriving expected counts from `AXIS_TIER_COUNTS`/`bankQuestionCount` instead of hardcoded numbers) + full `check:ota-gate` (59/59) all green, run before and after the reviewer-fix pass. No schema/auth/dependency/env changes. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Trait system redesign — first vertical slice (T-01 through T-04) shipped: Kalman-gain trait updates, tiered axis-priority question generation.** Full plan captured in `docs/archive/TRAIT_SYSTEM_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` (9 phases, "final agreed design" from a prior conversation, not yet built end to end) plus a companion `docs/archive/LEGENDS_ARCHETYPES_DRAFT.md` (64-archetype naming/theme layer — explicitly a draft, not started, blocked on its own open item about match-scoring weights). This entry covers only the foundational, no-schema first slice from the plan's §12 build order; phases 5-9 (progressive-unlock UI + new per-user schema, token economy, RCI, the roll/reveal mechanic, history views, Story move) are a separate, not-yet-planned next slice, each needing its own schema shown and approved individually per this repo's standing rule. Before building, delegated to `investigator` to re-check the plan's file-shape assumptions against current code (the repo moved since the plan was drafted, most recently the 2026-09-08 category-per-screen Questions UI and the 2026-09-07 revert of category batches from 10-question/chunked to 5-question/single-call) — 8 of 10 assumptions held, 2 had drifted: (1) the original multi-call chunked-generation pattern the plan wanted to reuse no longer exists anywhere in the tree (reverted along with the 10-question category-batch feature), needed a rebuild from scratch; (2) `CategoryPagedQuestions` hard-assumes real `CategoryDef` objects, not a generic category-shaped prop, so it can't be fed axis tiers directly as originally hoped (left for the next slice's UI work). **T-01 (`src/lib/trait-stability.ts`, `applyEwmaAnswer`):** replaced the fixed `EWMA_ALPHA` value-update with a Kalman gain derived from `prior_variance = max(KALMAN_MIN_VARIANCE, 1 - stability)` — a settled axis's next answer now moves `value` less than an early answer did (the plan's motivating problem: a 10-answer-settled axis used to swing exactly as much as a 2nd answer from one contradicting response). `stability` itself deliberately keeps its original, byte-unchanged agreement-based formula rather than the plan's literal Kalman-variance formula for stability — found via real test breakage, not by choice: a first attempt using pure Kalman-variance stability had no disagreement term and would have silently disabled `isInconsistentAnswerer`'s trap detection (a shipped Phase 6 feature, proven by an adversarial-answerer test in `trait-stability-check.ts`); confirmed with emci via AskUserQuestion mid-build before proceeding on the hybrid. `KALMAN_MEASUREMENT_NOISE` is calibrated to `(1-EWMA_ALPHA)/EWMA_ALPHA` (not an arbitrary guess) so the Kalman gain equals `EWMA_ALPHA` exactly whenever `stability===0` — value's update is mathematically equivalent to the old formula for any not-yet-settled axis. A second, less obvious regression surfaced after that fix: an arbitrary first-guess noise constant gave fresh axes a much higher gain (0.87) than `EWMA_ALPHA` (0.35) ever did, overshooting on ordinary varying (not just adversarial) answer sequences and, since `value` feeds into the next answer's delta/agreement, suppressing `stability` for a case that used to settle fine (caught by `wave20-check.ts`'s alternating-answer fixture) — root-caused and fixed via the calibration above, not by loosening any test. **Consequence flagged in the plan doc itself** (not just here): §5's RCI phase (not yet built) claimed `standard_error` and this section's `variance` are "in sync by construction" — no longer literally true, since there's no persisted Bayesian variance separate from `stability`; whoever builds RCI needs to derive `standard_error` from `stability` directly. **T-02** (`src/lib/questions/tiered-axis-plan.ts`): the §2 tier allocation (25 questions/round: 2 axes×3 + 1 axis×3 + 3 axes×2 + 10 axes×1), coverage enforced at the TypeScript level (`Record<TraitAxis, number>` fails to compile if an axis is missing). **T-03** (`src/lib/questions/chunked-generate.ts`): rebuilt the save-immediately/retry-shortfall-only multi-call generator the drift-check found missing, deliberately NOT re-added to `category-batch.ts` (that file stays intentionally single-call for its own feature) — new, separate infrastructure. Reviewer caught two real bugs before ship: intra-batch near-duplicates weren't filtered (two identical drafts from the same call both slipped through, since neither was in the exclusion list yet), and over-delivery wasn't capped (a call returning more drafts for one axis than requested would over-save past the round's target) — both fixed with a per-axis slot cap plus intra-attempt dedup, with new test coverage for both. **T-04** (`src/lib/questions/ongoing-round.ts`): composes one post-Full-Profile ongoing tiered round, grounded via the existing `pickQuestionGrounding` (not a new mechanism) — not yet wired into any live route/UI, since the 50-question intake it triggers after doesn't exist yet (next slice). Reviewer's other findings, left as documented follow-ups rather than fixed now (out of this slice's approved scope): `src/lib/questions/answer.ts`'s `updateTraitTrackValueOnly` (secondary-axis value nudges, pre-existing/Phase-4 code, untouched) now interacts with the Kalman gain in a subtly different way than the old fixed-alpha formula did — judged an accepted, arguably-intended consequence of confidence-based responsiveness, not a bug; none of the three new `src/lib/questions/*` modules have any consumer yet (expected — this slice is measurement/generation infra, not UI, per the approved plan). Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, no new warnings) + 4 new checks (`check:kalman` 7/7, `check:tiered-axis-plan` 4/4, `check:chunked-generate` 11/11, `check:ongoing-round` 1/1) + full `check:ota-gate` (57/57) all green, run repeatedly through both regressions and their fixes. No schema/auth/dependency/env changes. Pushed to `master`.

- 2026-09-08: **Full Profile ("A few questions") re-platformed from one flat 16-axis scroll to a reusable category-per-screen component, `CategoryPagedQuestions` (`src/components/category-paged-questions.tsx`).** Investigated read-only first (no code touched): confirmed the old `FullProfileList` was a single ungrouped `View.map()` over all 16 `TRAIT_AXES`; confirmed category membership was never a stored field (`QuestionDraft.category` exists but is explicitly documented as unused for grouping) — it's always a derived `CategoryDef.axes.includes(question.axis)` lookup, reused as-is per emci's explicit requirement, no new field added. New component takes `categories`, a caller-supplied `rowsForAxis(axis)` accessor, and a `storageKey` — genuinely reusable (no hardcoded question source), built for this and the future "questions stack" feature to share without copy-paste. Per-category screen: Back/Next/Skip navigation, "Category X of Y", and an "N of M axes complete" count built from `uniqueCategoryAxes` (dedupes an axis referenced by multiple categories — e.g. `extraversion` in both "Openness to life" and "Everyday social energy" — to count once, not once per category). Position (which category you're on) persists via a new `category-page-position.ts` (AsyncStorage, same per-device tradeoff `full-profile-unlock.ts` already accepts) so leaving and returning restores the same category. **The answer-save path was not touched at all** — `pickBankItem`/`applyQuestionAnswer` reused byte-identical, only the surrounding read/render logic changed. **This reverses a documented Sep 2026 decision**: `scripts/questions-check.ts` used to assert "no category picker UI... every axis is always shown, no per-category chips" as a regression guard — that assertion was rewritten to test the new (opposite) behavior rather than left stale/silently-false. Two real things caught mid-build and fixed: (1) `AsyncStorage.getItem/setItem` throws `"window is not defined"` under plain Node (confirmed via a manual probe) — `category-page-position.ts` got an in-memory `Map` cache alongside the storage attempt, same pattern `full-profile-unlock.ts` already uses, so within-session restore still works even when the real write fails; (2) importing the `.tsx` component (which imports `react-native`) directly into a Node check script fails the same way `growth.ts` once did — the dedup/progress math (`uniqueCategoryAxes`, `completedAxesFrom`) was split into a react-native-free `src/lib/questions/category-paged.ts`, with the component re-exporting rather than redefining, so there's exactly one implementation. Reviewer's one real finding: the component was fed a mount-time `getCategoryDefs()` snapshot instead of the live-subscribed `useCategoryDefs()` hook (same one `categories-fold.tsx` already uses) — fixed, plus restored a guard the old check accidentally dropped (`CategoryPagedQuestions`/Full Profile's bank wiring must never reach `routeQuestions`/`priorityAxes`/`mergeCategoryPriority` — those stay the default rotation's concern only) and cleaned up two now-dead style keys plus a stale doc-comment reference to the deleted function. Emci's own 4 explicit verification requirements were built as real, non-mocked assertions in a new `check:category-paged-questions` script (not just "it compiles"): **(1) answer persists across Back navigation — PASS** (`rowsForAxis` is proven pure w.r.t. navigation: identical output before/after, since it closes over `tracks` alone, never the category index); **(2) shared-axis progress never double-counts — PASS** (a real axis shared by 2+ live categories, fully answered, contributes exactly 1 to the completion count, not 2); **(3) a 3-question and a 5-question category both render without cutoff — PASS** (structural check: no `maxHeight`/`numberOfLines`/`overflow:hidden`/`.slice()` truncation anywhere in the component; content scrolls via the parent screen's existing `ScrollView`, same as before); **(4) leaving and returning restores the same category — PASS** (position save→load round-trip via the in-memory cache, scoped independently per `storageKey` so a future second question set can't collide with Full Profile's). Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, no new warnings) + `check:questions` (41/41, rewritten) + new `check:category-paged-questions` (4/4) + full `check:ota-gate` (53/53) all green, run twice (before and after the reviewer-fix pass). Pushed to `master`.


- 2026-09-07: **"10 questions per category" simplified down to 5 per category, per emci's explicit call.** Context: while working through applying the wave44 migration live, confirmed via `list_migrations` that it had never actually been applied (interrupted before that step) — so there was no live table to alter; the size change was made directly in the still-unapplied `wave44_category_question_batches.sql` rather than by creating a second "tightening" migration against a nonexistent table, correcting a factual premise in the initial ask. `CATEGORY_BATCH_SIZE` is now 5 everywhere (`category-batch.ts`, `sort_index < 5` / `entry_count > 5` / `count(*) >= 5` in the migration's schema and all three RPCs, `category-batch-check.ts`). **The chunking/resume machinery from the 10-question version is gone entirely** — `composeCategoryBatch` is now a single `generateQuestionBatch` call for all 5, no `MAX_PER_CALL`/`MAX_ATTEMPTS` loop, no `existing`/resume parameter, no partial-save-then-retry-for-remainder logic; a short result from that one call is just saved as-is (no top-up), and `CategoryBatchFold`'s resume check (`existing.items.length >= CATEGORY_BATCH_SIZE`) is now the only resume-adjacent code left — if a batch is short, reopening it regenerates from scratch rather than topping up, an accepted limitation since 5-in-one-call is expected to reliably succeed (it's the same size Infinite Questions already uses). `categoryBatchAxisPlan`'s filler cap is `floor(5/2) = 2` (was `floor(10/2) = 5`), same reused-least-answered-signal logic, unresized elsewhere. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors) + `check:category-batch` (15/15, rewritten for the single-call flow) + full `check:ota-gate` (52/52) all green before push. **wave44 (5-per-category) applied to the live `ato` project** (migration `20260908031147`, confirmed via `list_migrations`; security advisor showed only the expected INFO-level SECURITY DEFINER notices on the three new RPCs, same pattern as `question_items`'s existing RPCs, no RLS-missing-policy findings). **Published to production OTA** via `npm run ota:publish -- --branch production` (gate green), commit `cb1ebbf`, update group `90999796-38a4-4e81-bfb7-94efdf0435e9`.


- 2026-09-07: **"10 questions per category" shipped end to end — new mode, new schema (file created, not applied), lock, and cross-category Submit/Cancel.** Built from a fully investigated/reviewed plan (six rounds of read-only investigation before any code): `QuestionsFold`'s existing-but-unwired `category` prop is the entry point, via a new sibling component `CategoryBatchFold` (kept separate from `QuestionsFold` itself so the existing Infinite Questions rotation/skip/checkpoint code is completely untouched). **New per-category closed-set state** — `category_question_batches`/`category_question_items` (`supabase/migrations/wave44_category_question_batches.sql`, own RPCs: `insert_category_batch_items`, `answer_category_question_item`, `finalize_category_batches`) — deliberately NOT built on the existing `question_packs`/`question_items` tables, which have a hard `sort_index < 5`/`entry_count between 1 and 5` constraint that can't hold 10 items; found mid-build, corrected schema shown to and approved by emci before writing the migration. **Composition** (`categoryBatchAxisPlan` in `src/lib/questions/category-batch.ts`): category's own axes first (round-robin), then single-axis filler questions for lagging axes elsewhere (`effectiveStability === 0`, reusing the existing least-answered signal), capped at half the batch (5 of 10) and never more than the number of actually-lagging axes — a self-authored, documented rule since the product spec didn't fix an exact cutoff. **Generation is chunked, not single-shot:** first-pass review caught that `ai-generate`'s Edge Function hard-clamps `maxOutputTokens` to 1024 server-side regardless of what a client requests (this repo's existing hard invariant), so asking for all 10 in one call risked truncation — every `generateQuestionBatch` call here is capped at 5 (the proven-safe size already used by Infinite Questions), and 10 are assembled across as many calls as needed via a save-immediately/retry-only-the-shortfall loop, with exact-duplicate-text exclusion against both `question_items` history and the batch's own already-saved questions. Same review also caught that resuming a partial batch was broken (hardcoded `remaining = 10` regardless of what was already saved, so reopening a 6/10 category would always hit the RPC's 10-item ceiling and get permanently stuck) — fixed by threading the already-fetched batch into `composeCategoryBatch` as a resume seed. Lock at 10/10 mirrors Full Profile's existing `fullProfileLocked` pattern exactly (read-only in place, no nav/toast). `categories-fold.tsx` gained per-category batch links plus a Submit/Cancel prompt shown once every real category (`getCategoryDefs()`) reports locked; Submit calls `finalizeCategoryBatches()` (sets `finalized_at`, nothing else); Cancel just dismisses the prompt locally, no reopening logic, per the approved spec. Two full reviewer passes: first caught the token-cap and resume bugs above (both fixed) plus an ungated draft-copy banner, a Submit/Cancel row missing `flexDirection: 'row'` (buttons were stacking vertically), a duplicated draft-copy banner, and unbounded growth on the history-exclusion query (capped at 500, newest-first) — all fixed; second pass came back clean. New `check:category-batch` (15 assertions, picked up automatically by `check:ota-gate`); existing `check:questions` (40/40) and the full 52-check gate stayed green throughout, typecheck/lint clean. **Left as known follow-ups, not done:** answering an item writes the DB row before the trait value (same ordering as the pre-existing pack `pick()` in `questions-fold.tsx` — if the trait write fails, the item is permanently marked answered with no retry path, mirrors an existing accepted tradeoff, not a new one); `CategoriesFold`'s `tracks` isn't refetched after an in-place batch answer, so its category readings can go stale until remount; the error state offers no retry button; `docs/MAP.md`/`docs/NOW.md`/`docs/FLOWS.md` don't yet have entries for the three new files or wave44. **Migration file created but NOT applied to the live DB** — needs emci to review and apply via Supabase SQL editor/CLI, same as prior schema-only commits. Pushed to `master` (`df5418e`).

- 2026-09-07: **One-time "Legends unlocked!" celebration shipped, wired into legends.tsx directly rather than through intake-sweep.tsx's crossedMilestonesFor.** New `legends_unlocked` def (metric `profile_settled`, threshold 1) in `MILESTONE_DEFS` — the first milestone in this feature not driven by the Questions-tab pipeline: it fires on `isProfileSettled(tracks)` going false→true, which is only meaningfully computed on the Legends tab (`const locked = tracksReady && !isProfileSettled(tracks);`, left byte-identical — `isProfileSettled` itself and its ~13 other call sites untouched). Reuses the exact same `celebrated_milestone_ids`/`checkMilestones`/`persistCelebratedMilestones`/`MilestoneToast` mechanism as every other milestone, just wired locally in `legends.tsx` instead of through `crossedMilestonesFor`. **Reviewer caught a critical bug before push:** the celebration effect originally gated on `if (!me || locked || ...) return;` — but `locked` reads `false` both when the profile is genuinely settled AND while `tracksReady` is still `false` (the initial state on every mount, and reset again whenever `me.updated_at` changes), so the bug would have fired — and permanently persisted — the celebration for literally any user, including a brand-new unsettled profile, during the loading window before the first real tracks fetch resolved. Fixed by also gating on `tracksReady` (`if (!me || !tracksReady || locked || ...) return;`) and adding it to the effect's dependency array; re-review confirmed the fix is correct via the full truth table (loading → return; unsettled → return; settled → fires once) and that no other code path can reach the celebration during the loading window. Also fixed alongside: a failed `persistCelebratedMilestones` call now resets the retry ref (previously stayed permanently blocked on a network error, unlike `intake-sweep.tsx`'s backfill effect which already resets), and a brittle multi-line string anchor in `check:milestones` (would have broken on a CRLF checkout, this repo has no `.gitattributes` forcing LF) was replaced with a single-line one. `check:milestones` grew from 23 to 28 assertions, including a wiring-proof fixture (a real settled-profile `TraitTrack[]` run through the actual `isProfileSettled`, not `checkMilestones` fed a hand-picked 1) and a regression guard pinning the exact fixed guard-condition string so the critical bug can't silently reappear. Flagged, not yet done: the copy ("Legends unlocked!" / "Your profile is settled — Legends are ready for you.") is new user-facing text Emci hasn't reviewed, same placeholder-copy status as every other MILESTONE_DEFS entry so far. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 51 offline checks green, run before and after the critical fix, with a full re-review pass in between (not just a fix-and-ship). Pushed to `master` (`e541be0`). `crossedMilestonesFor`/`intake-sweep.tsx` and the separate, unrelated `full-profile-unlock.ts`/`FullProfileUnlockAck` capstone celebration remain untouched.

- 2026-09-06: **Real day-streak milestones shipped (streak_3/7/21) and the dormant one-time presence-milestone celebration fully retired.** Investigated first (read-only, two passes): confirmed check-ins are recorded with real per-day granularity (`checks.logged_on`), confirmed timezone-safe day-math utilities already existed (`localYmd`/`addDaysYmd`/`daysBetweenYmd`) plus a near-identical consecutive-day pattern already shipped (`hasWeekWithoutCut` in `badges.ts`), and confirmed NavPixel's celebration is a text-free scale/hand-pose animation on a fixed nav icon — not a realistic `MilestoneToast` swap, so the new streak milestones use the existing toast pipeline instead of touching NavPixel's animation shape. **New `computeStreak(checks, timezone, now?)`** in `growth.ts`: builds a distinct-calendar-day set keyed off `logged_on` (falling back to `created_at`, mirroring `checks.ts`'s `checkLoggedOnYmd` — but reimplemented locally as `loggedOnYmd`, not imported, for a reason caught mid-build: importing the real `checkLoggedOnYmd` pulled `checks.ts`'s Supabase-client import chain — and therefore `react-native` — into `growth.ts`, breaking that file's documented "free of react-native, Node-testable" property; `npx tsx scripts/growth-check.ts` failed with an esbuild transform error on `node_modules/react-native/index.js` until fixed). Walks backward from today counting consecutive present days, recomputed fresh every call (never an incremental counter) so a backdated check (record_check allows up to 2 days late) correctly heals a gap on the very next call instead of requiring reconciliation logic. **New `hoursSinceLocalMidnight` in `local-date.ts`** backs a `STREAK_GRACE_HOURS = 3` grace window — genuinely new, nothing like it existed before: a not-yet-logged "today" still counts as continuing yesterday's streak within 3 hours of local midnight. `streak_3/7/21` added to `MILESTONE_DEFS` (metric `current_streak`) and wired into `crossedMilestonesFor`'s existing two call sites; the backfill effect's guard also gained `checksReady` (previously `tracksReady` only) — closing a race where it could mark itself done against an empty `checks` array before real checks load, then wrongly treat a real user's pre-existing streak as newly-crossed on their next answer. **Retirement, approved for outright deletion (confirmed zero real-account firings across two investigation passes):** `milestones_celebrated`, `markMilestoneCelebrated`, `PRESENCE_MILESTONES`, `shouldCelebrateMilestone`, and NavPixel's celebration effect + the `celebrateRef` it wired — all removed. `useGrowth()` now returns just `{ state }` (was `{ state, pendingMilestone, markCelebrated }`). Confirmed via a dedicated boundary investigation that the LIVE ambient presence-tier glow (`presenceTier`, `PRESENCE_TIERS`, `growthState`, `GrowthState`, `presenceGlowLayersForTier`, `neonGlowColors`, `PRESENCE_GLOW_LAYERS`) and NavPixel's tap-mood/daily-gesture animations are structurally separate and fully untouched; `PixelFace`'s `celebrateRef` prop itself stays (still used independently by `pixel-lab.tsx` for dev testing) — only NavPixel's own instance/wiring of it was removed. **Per Emci's explicit call: the `public.me.milestones_celebrated` DB column is deliberately left in place**, orphaned but harmless — no migration touched. Reviewer's one real finding (a blank `me.timezone` would throw a `RangeError` inside `computeStreak`) fixed with the same `|| 'UTC'` fallback this codebase already uses elsewhere (`me.ts`). `check:milestones` grew from 19 to 23 assertions (streak config + a real `Check[]`/`computeStreak` wiring-proof fixture, mirroring the axis-complete one); `growth-check.ts`'s celebration-only assertions were replaced with 7 new `computeStreak`/grace-period/blank-timezone assertions. `docs/DATA_AI_MAP.md`'s row for the column updated to note the retirement. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 51 offline checks green, run before and after the timezone fix. Pushed to `master` (`189176c`). `isProfileSettled`, `celebrated_milestone_ids`, `checkMilestones`, `axis_complete_*`, and the persistent badge-chip system are all untouched. **Milestone feature has no open items right now** — every metric (bankTotalProgress, profile_percent, axisComplete, current_streak) is defined AND wired.

- 2026-09-06: **axis_complete_* MILESTONE_DEFS wired into crossedMilestonesFor — every part of the milestone feature (T-01 through the per-axis defs) is now live, nothing left config-only.** `crossedMilestonesFor` (`intake-sweep.tsx`) gained a `TRAIT_AXES.flatMap` loop calling `checkMilestones(\`axisComplete:${axis}\`, axisVariant(tracks, axis), celebrated)` per axis, spread into the same merged list as `bankTotalProgress`/`profile_percent` — both existing call sites (T-04 backfill, T-05 `refreshAfterAnswer`) pick it up automatically, no new call sites needed. Reviewer confirmed the backfill effect (unchanged) still gates both Questions UI blocks behind `backfillReady`, so a user who already completed an axis before this shipped gets it silently absorbed on first mount — no surprise toast for old progress, same guarantee `bankTotalProgress`/`profile_percent` already had. **Confirmed unit-consistent:** `axisVariant`'s report-track `answerCount` and the threshold (`bankQuestionCount([axis])`, that axis's own bank size) measure the same thing for a single axis, so no cap is needed the way `bankTotalProgress` needs one when summing across all 16. Per Emci's explicit ask, `check:milestones` gained a genuine wiring-proof fixture — a real constructed `TraitTrack` run through the actual `axisVariant` function (not `checkMilestones` fed a hand-picked number, which only proved the config/generic-filter logic, not the wire from tracks to milestone) — confirming completing an axis crosses it, one answer short doesn't, an already-celebrated one doesn't re-cross. Reviewer's one real coverage gap (a `game`-track/gut-call fixture wasn't proven to NOT count, even though `axisVariant` reads the report track only) was fixed by adding that exact assertion. Reviewer's other note — `axisVariant` counts any report-track answer, not strictly "bank" answers, so the "axis complete" copy is very slightly stronger than what's measured — was left as a documentation-worthy observation, not a code change (matches how `bankTotalProgress` already uses the same function the same way). `check:milestones` grew from 18 to 19 assertions (net; the new fixture block replaced/extended prior coverage). Gate: typecheck + lint + all 51 offline checks green, run before and after the game-track fix. Pushed to `master` (`f3580fa`). `isProfileSettled`, `milestones_celebrated`, the badge-chip system, and `src/lib/milestones.ts` itself (only the caller changed) remain untouched. **Milestone feature has no open items right now.**

- 2026-09-06: **profile_100 and the bank_percent metric removed entirely, closing the open item from the entry directly below.** Emci's call after the duplicate-toast finding: rather than merge or reword the pair, drop `profile_100` outright — it was redundant with `answers_48` (both fire on "every bank question answered"). Since `profile_100` was `bank_percent`'s only consumer, the metric itself was removed too; `MilestoneMetric` reverted to `'bankTotalProgress' | 'profile_percent' | \`axisComplete:${TraitAxis}\``. `profile_50` (still on `profile_percent`) is untouched — reviewer confirmed it's byte-identical across this whole arc, and `crossedMilestonesFor`/its two call sites are byte-identical to how they worked before `bank_percent` was ever introduced (a clean revert, not a subtly different reimplementation). `check:milestones` gained a negative assertion (`!MILESTONE_DEFS.some(d => d.id === 'profile_100')`) so a future re-add under any metric name gets caught, not just under the old one.
