Separately, `user_legend_history` table comments in `wave25_legends_archetypes.sql:167` and `wave32_legend_variants.sql:125` mention "weekly batch" — this is **serving/never-repeat bookkeeping only** (`week_batch_id` for which batch a variant was shown in), unrelated to content generation; do not conflate the two "weekly batch" usages. [docs/red-team-2026-09-03.md](docs/red-team-2026-09-03.md) finding **F3 "Only four Legend figures"** (line 674) independently flags the same 4-of-12 content gap as a scale risk ("Legends is the most shareable thing in the app and it will collide immediately") and recommends "Get to 20+ figures before Legends is promoted anywhere. This is content work, not [code]." **Net: Legends generation is a written spec and a content-scale problem, not a hidden or half-built code path.** No code changed.

- 2026-09-04: **Audit, read-only, follow-up: a reusable per-user ledger pattern already exists (`ai_usage` / `claim_ai_call`) and a reusable pg_cron pattern already exists — a legend-token weekly-grant system would extend both rather than invent new infra.** Emci asked two specific questions ahead of possibly designing a legend-token system. **(1) Existing balance/ledger pattern for AI spend — yes, `ai_usage`.** [supabase/migrations/stage8_ai_quota.sql](supabase/migrations/stage8_ai_quota.sql) is the live pattern behind the 20/day-200/month AI router cap (`docs/ME.md`'s "Floor requirements"): `public.ai_usage(user_id, day, calls)` — a per-user-per-UTC-day counter, PK `(user_id, day)`, RLS `select`-own only (no client write policy at all). All writes funnel through one `security definer` RPC, `claim_ai_call()`: reads caps from `app_config` (`ai_daily_cap`/`ai_monthly_cap`, config-driven, no rebuild needed), takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(uid::text))` to serialize concurrent claims for the same user (closing the two-in-flight-requests-both-pass-a-cap-of-1 race), upserts today's row, sums the trailing month, and returns `{ok, reason, daily, daily_cap, monthly, monthly_cap}` — the client never decides whether a call happens (matches this repo's hard invariant in `CLAUDE.md`, "quota is claimed server-side"). This exact shape generalizes directly to a legend-token ledger: a `legend_tokens` (or reuse `ai_usage`-style) table keyed `(user_id, period)` with a `claim_legend_token()` RPC doing the same select-own RLS + advisory-lock + atomic-decrement dance, rather than a new balance mechanism. `me.tokens` (referenced in `docs/BUSINESS.md`: "Notes are earned only... they never paywall viewing Full Profile") is a **different**, adjacent currency — the in-app "Notes" economy for Sage spend/teaching — worth knowing about so a new "legend token" name doesn't collide with it in copy or code. **(2) Existing weekly/scheduled-job pattern for Supabase functions — yes, pg_cron, but currently used for a different cadence and never yet wired to an Edge Function.** Two real precedents: `dev_access.sql:521-536` schedules an **hourly** job (`cron.schedule('dev-trace-expire', '17 * * * *', $$select public.purge_expired_dev_trace();$$)`) that calls a plain SQL function directly — wrapped in a `do $cron$ ... exception when others then raise notice` block so a Postgres install without `pg_cron` available doesn't fail the migration, and unschedules-then-reschedules so it's idempotent across repeated migration runs. Separately, `wave2_around_city.sql:35-40` sets up (`create extension if not exists pg_net`, `create extension if not exists pg_cron`) for a **twice-daily** refresh of the `refresh-around` Edge Function (Edmtrain event cache), explicitly stating the job itself is "created in a follow-up SQL once `around_refresh_secret` lives in Vault" — and per `docs/NOW.md:219`, **that follow-up was never done**: "cron not scheduled until both exist" (`EDMTRAIN_CLIENT_KEY` + `AROUND_REFRESH_SECRET`). So there is no live example in this repo of pg_cron actually invoking an Edge Function over HTTP yet (only cron calling a SQL function directly, as `dev-trace-expire` does) — a weekly legend-grant job could go the simpler proven route (a `security definer` SQL function scheduled directly via `cron.schedule`, no `pg_net`/Edge Function/Vault secret needed at all, since granting tokens is a pure DB write) or become the repo's first cron→Edge-Function wiring if the grant needs to call an external API. No code changed.

- 2026-09-04: **Questions-tab category picker redesigned: a category now shows a bank-driven browsable list with sequential per-axis unlock, chip question counts, and a total-progress header — replacing last session's `priorityAxes`-routed one-at-a-time picker entirely.** Requested as a bigger follow-up once the picker's routing bug (see the earlier fix entry, same day) was patched — the fix made the picker correctly serve the right axis, but emci wanted a genuinely different interaction: browse a category's full question set instead of stepping through it one at a time via rotation. Design was shown and approved before building (screen layout + a real technical tradeoff flagged up front): since nothing in the schema tracks *which exact* bank question a user answered (only a running per-axis answer count in `trait_history`), an accurate "N of 48 answered" and per-item answered state requires either (A) treating each axis's 3 bank drafts as unlocking strictly in order — accurate by construction from the existing `axisVariant`/`bankDraftFor` cycling, zero schema change — or (B) a new persisted table for free-order answering, which would need a schema change and sign-off. **Emci chose A.** New pure helpers in [src/lib/questions/local.ts](src/lib/questions/local.ts): `bankProgressForAxis`/`bankProgressForAxes` (per-draft `'answered' | 'current' | 'locked'` state, `index < answerCount` / `== answerCount` / `> answerCount`, capped at the list length so cycling past 3 answers stays fully-answered rather than re-locking), `bankQuestionCount` (real per-category count — 6 or 9 depending on axis count, not a fixed number), `bankTotalProgress` (`{answered, total}` summed over all 16 axes, `total` always `QUESTIONS_BANK.length` = 48). [src/components/questions-fold.tsx](src/components/questions-fold.tsx): picking a category now renders a new `CategoryQuestionsList` component built straight from `bankProgressForAxes` — **not routed through `routeQuestions`/`priorityAxes` at all**, which sidesteps the entire bug class from the earlier same-day fix rather than depending on it; `mergeCategoryPriority` wiring removed from `load()` (the helper itself stays in `deferral.ts`, still tested, just unused by this component now). Each axis's 3 drafts unlock independently of every other axis's progress. Skip-this/skip-the-rest/the pause-after-5-answers checkpoint are absent from the category list by design (per emci's explicit scope) — they stay exactly as before on the default rotation, which is completely unchanged and remains the entry point when no category is selected. Fail-open preserved: `getCategoryDefs()` throwing, or a stale `selectedCategory` that no longer resolves to a def, makes `activeCategory` read `null`, which falls straight through to the untouched default-rotation chain — same rule as before, no new failure mode. Coverage: `check:questions` 29→31 — sequential-unlock states at 0/1/2/3/7 answers (including the no-overflow/no-re-lock case), per-axis independence, count/progress math, and two source-scoped assertions (the category-list function body excludes skip/pause copy constants; the default-rotation section above it still contains them) so a future edit to either branch can't silently regress the other. Gate: typecheck + lint (0 errors, only pre-existing unrelated warnings) + all 50 offline checks green. Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified.

- 2026-09-04: **Legends gated behind `isProfileSettled`, same pattern as the other locked surfaces.** [src/app/(tabs)/legends.tsx](src/app/(tabs)/legends.tsx) — the screen that renders matched `LegendCard`s — now computes `locked = tracksReady && !isProfileSettled(tracks)` and, when locked, shows `PROFILE_LOCKED_COPY` ("Complete your profile to unlock this") plus a `PROFILE_LOCKED_CTA` link to `/intake-sweep` (deep-linked to the missing axis via `missingAxis`, same helper the existing thin-profile empty state already used) instead of matched cards, the thin-profile card, or the plain empty state — mirrors `sage-title-card.tsx`'s locked block byte-for-byte in structure. Gated only after `tracksReady` so the screen doesn't flash locked before it knows better, matching `sage-title-card.tsx`'s `tracksReady` guard. **Legends joins Explore observations, Sage Title, and Sage insight as a surface that locks with copy** (verified by grepping every live call site of `PROFILE_LOCKED_COPY` — `legends.tsx`, `explore.tsx`, `sage-title-card.tsx`, `sage-insight-spend.tsx`; `explore-panel.tsx` also matches but is a known dead-code duplicate of `explore.tsx`, not a second live surface) — **4 locked surfaces total**, distinct from the daily card and Sage chat, which use the same `isProfileSettled` predicate but degrade silently to local content rather than showing a lock. **Matching itself is untouched** — still the static archetype pool (`buildLegendView`), no model call and no quota spend either way, locked or not; this change only decides what renders. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green. Reviewer: not run (no reviewer subagent invoked this session). Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified.

- 2026-09-04: **Fixed: Questions-tab category picker highlighted a category but no question changed — `routeQuestions` was returning today's already-open cached item before ever consulting `priorityAxes`.** Root cause, confirmed by the `investigator` subagent tracing the full picker → axis-resolution → `mergeCategoryPriority` → `routeQuestions` chain: the picker, `getCategoryDefs()` lookup, and `mergeCategoryPriority` were all correct and correctly wired (no dead state, no id/label mismatch) — the break was entirely inside `routeQuestions` ([src/lib/questions/route.ts](src/lib/questions/route.ts)), which served `nextPlayableItem(todays)` — the first open item in today's pack, in raw item order — immediately, before `input.priorityAxes` (computed 30+ lines further down) was ever read. Since the Questions tab almost always already has an open pack/item by the time a category is tapped, every pick just re-served the same stale question regardless of category. **Two-attempt fix, both required** (first review pass caught a real gap in attempt 1): (1) new `openItemForAxes(pack, axes)` searches today's pack for an open item whose axis is in the wanted list (same `isOpenQuestionItem` + `questionDraftGuardHit == null` predicate `nextPlayableItem` already used) and is tried first when `priorityAxes` is non-empty. (2) **Attempt 1 alone was incomplete** — reviewer flagged that when no open item in today's pack matched the priority (routine, since a category maps to 2-3 of 16 axes against a 5-item pack), the code still fell back to serving an unrelated open item, reproducing the exact reported symptom in a large fraction of cases. Fixed by making a priority miss fall through to the pack's existing regeneration path (the same one already used whenever there's no open item at all) instead of falling back — that path already reads `priorityAxes` correctly and already respects the existing profile-completeness/quota-claim gating, so this reuses existing spend logic rather than adding new spend behavior. Second reviewer pass confirmed the fall-through isn't dropped a second time and traced the bounded cost (regeneration now fires only when `priorityAxes` is non-empty — category pick, `focusAxis` deep-link, or deferred axes — not on the default empty-priority path every user hits on a normal open). Two new regression assertions in `check:questions` (27→29): a priority axis WITH a matching open item is served over item order; a priority axis with NO matching open item regenerates a fresh batch led by that axis rather than serving the stale unrelated item. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green, run after each attempt. Reviewer: FAIL (critical gap) → fix → PASS. Files: [src/lib/questions/route.ts](src/lib/questions/route.ts), [scripts/questions-check.ts](scripts/questions-check.ts). Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified. This same fix also corrects the pre-existing `focusAxis` deep-link (e.g. from Legends), which shared the identical broken code path — not new scope, same root cause.

- 2026-09-04: **Audit, read-only: question-engine architecture — current implementation vs. a target multi-axis/weighted/adaptive spec (`primaryAxes`/`secondaryAxes`/`excludedAxes`, `categoryId` as conceptual home not axis-bucket, confidence tracked separately from score, adaptive selection, contradiction handling). Decision: targeted additive upgrades, not a full rebuild.** Compared the spec concept-by-concept against `QuestionDraft` ([types.ts](src/lib/questions/types.ts)), the 48-question bank ([bank.ts](src/lib/questions/bank.ts)), `TraitTrack`/`effectiveStability`/`isProfileSettled` ([trait-stability.ts](src/lib/trait-stability.ts)), `CATEGORY_DEFS` ([categories.ts](src/lib/categories.ts)), and the AI generator's prompt/parser ([prompt.ts](src/lib/questions/prompt.ts), [parse.ts](src/lib/questions/parse.ts)). **Exists:** the 16-axis `TraitTrack` model (`value`/`stability`/`answerCount`) already matches the spec's "trait axes are the scoring source of truth"; `CATEGORY_DEFS`' axis membership already matches the spec's category list almost exactly; `stability` (decayed, floored per the Sep 4 floor fix above) already plays confidence's role. **Doesn't exist:** every bank/AI-generated `QuestionDraft` is single-axis only (`parse.ts` enforces exactly one axis) — no `primaryAxes`/`secondaryAxes`/`excludedAxes`/`redundancyTags`/weight/reason anywhere (confirmed by grep); no `directEvidenceCount`/`totalEvidenceCount` split or `evidenceHistory` log (only one `answerCount`); no confidence-driven adaptive selection (`route.ts`'s `guardedBatch` keys on unfilled-axes-first + freshness rotation, never on `stability`); no contradiction-handling subsystem at all. **Additive-vs-structural split:** extending `QuestionDraft` with the new optional fields, extending the AI prompt/parser to request the richer shape, and adding `directEvidenceCount`/`totalEvidenceCount`/`evidenceHistory` as new `TraitTrack` fields are all additive — existing single-axis entries degrade to `primaryAxes: [{axis, weight: 1}]`, nothing breaks. Looping the scoring-apply step (`mergeTraitWrite`/`updateTraits`) over primary+secondary axes is a logic change but keeps writing into the same `TraitTrack` fields. Layering a lowest-stability signal into `route.ts` selection is a moderate rework of an existing function, not a new one. Contradiction handling is a genuinely new subsystem with no current analog. **Recommendation: targeted upgrades.** (Phases 1-4 now shipped locally as of 2026-09-05 — see the entry below; Phase 5, confidence-aware selection, and Phase 6, contradiction handling, remain.) The real gap lives entirely in the question layer (schema → bank/generator → scoring-apply → selection); the trait-axis and category layers already match the spec closely enough that rewriting them would be pure risk for no gain. Critically, `isProfileSettled` ([trait-stability.ts:269](src/lib/trait-stability.ts:269)) and its 10 call sites across 9 files (`legends.tsx`, `check-milestone-badge.tsx`, `explore/route.ts`, `sage-insight.ts`, `sage-insight-spend.tsx`, `sage-title-card.tsx`, `voice/router.ts`, `voice/talk.ts`, `full-profile-unlock.ts`) never need to change under any additive path, since `TraitTrack.value`/`.stability`/`.answerCount` keep their current meaning throughout. Approved build order, one phase at a time with sign-off between each: (1) additive `QuestionDraft` schema extension, (2) additive `TraitTrack` evidence-count/history split, (3) additive AI prompt/parser extension, (4) scoring-apply loop over primary+secondary axes (same schema), (5) confidence-aware selection in `route.ts` (moderate rework), (6) contradiction handling as a separate later task, explicitly not started until 1-5 ship. No code changed for this entry; Phase 1 begins in the entry that follows.

- 2026-09-05: **Phase 4 shipped locally (not pushed — holding for one combined OTA push after all phases): scoring-apply now loops over `primaryAxes`+`secondaryAxes` when a `QuestionDraft` sets them, with a confidence-safety invariant proven by test, not just claimed.** Investigation first found `mergeTraitWrite`/`updateTraits` ([traits.ts:168](src/lib/traits.ts:168), [me.ts:519](src/lib/me.ts:519)) were ALREADY multi-axis capable (`incoming: Partial<Record<TraitAxis,number>>`, `allowed: TraitAxis[]`) — zero changes needed there. The only work was at the 3 call sites that build single-key objects from one axis. **Two are wired** (both answer directly off a `QuestionDraft`, never a persisted DB row): `intake-sweep.tsx`'s `pick()` and, found reachable mid-investigation despite an initial assumption otherwise, `questions-fold.tsx`'s `pickBankItem` (its `CategoryQuestionsList` caller already had the full `row.draft` in scope, just wasn't passing it through — extended `onPick`'s signature). **One is explicitly NOT wired and documented as such**: `questions-fold.tsx`'s `pick()`, keyed on a persisted `QuestionItemRow`, which has no axis-weight columns — adding them would be a `question_items` schema change, not done, not asked (comment left at the call site pointing here). New shared helper `applyQuestionAnswer` ([questions/answer.ts](src/lib/questions/answer.ts)) used by both wired call sites, so the primary/secondary logic exists in exactly one place. **Design decision surfaced and confirmed with emci before building:** naively feeding a weight-damped secondary value into the existing `applyEwmaAnswer` would make the EWMA see near-perfect self-agreement and inflate `stability` fast — the opposite of the spec's "secondary evidence alone must not create high confidence." Fixed by never routing secondary axes through `applyEwmaAnswer` at all: `nudgedSecondaryValue` ([trait-stability.ts](src/lib/trait-stability.ts)) computes a pure weight-scaled pull, persisted via a new narrow `updateTraitTrackValueOnly` ([trait-tracks-store.ts](src/lib/trait-tracks-store.ts)) that touches ONLY the `value` column — `stability`/`answer_count`/`last_touched`/`last_depth_at` are never touched by a secondary write, proven (not just asserted) in `check:trait-stability`'s new fixture: an axis genuinely settled before the write stays settled with numerically identical `effectiveStability` after. `totalEvidenceCount`/`evidenceHistory` (Phase 2) are populated via the existing `trait_history` table (per emci's explicit choice) — no new columns anywhere; primary axes are untouched, weight=1 fallback is byte-identical to the pre-Phase-4 single-axis write. **Two review passes, several real bugs caught and fixed before commit:** first pass (PASS, warnings only) found (a) a same-axis clobber if a malformed draft lists one axis in both `primaryAxes` and `secondaryAxes` — fixed via a `primaryAxisSet` exclusion + a `seenSecondary` dedupe Set; (b) `trait_history` rows were logging the raw incoming signal where every other writer (`historyDiff` in `me.ts`) logs the post-merge value — fixed to log the nudged value; (c) `insertTraitHistory` was unguarded (would abort the whole answer flow on a history-insert failure, after the trait write already committed) — wrapped in `.catch()` matching `persistMergedTraits`'s exact convention; (d) per-axis history inserts instead of one batched call — batched; (e) comments overclaimed total isolation from stability when a delayed indirect effect is real (a later primary answer on the same axis compares against the nudged value) — reworded to be accurate; (f) the new test was a tautology (fixture floor-blocked on both sides) — rewritten with a genuinely-settled fixture and a numeric `effectiveStability` equality assertion. Second pass (PASS) confirmed all of those, then caught one more: a secondary axis with no existing track was still logging a `trait_history` row claiming a move that never happened (the `trait_tracks` update was correctly a no-op, but the log write wasn't guarded the same way) — fixed by skipping the whole per-axis block (both the DB write and the history log) when no track exists yet, rather than logging only one half of a no-op. **Known, accepted, not fixed (inert today, flagged for later):** `excludedAxes` is parsed and documented but not enforced anywhere — a draft listing an axis in both `secondaryAxes` and `excludedAxes` would still get nudged; harmless today since no content sets `secondaryAxes` yet. **Reachability, still true after Phase 4:** this entire phase is inert in production — no bank entry or AI-generated draft currently sets `primaryAxes`/`secondaryAxes` (Phases 1-3 only added the capability to carry them). New assertions: `check:trait-stability` gained 3 (`nudgedSecondaryValue` boundary cases at weight 0/0.5/1, the settled-before/after invariant, `directEvidenceCountFor`/`totalEvidenceCountFor` default behavior). Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green, both before and after the review-driven fixes.

- 2026-09-05: **Phase 6 groundwork shipped locally (not pushed — same combined-OTA-push hold): T-01/T-02/T-03 built (detection + wiring), T-04 (real caller) explicitly skipped and left for a separate task — this phase is entirely inert, same pattern as Phases 1-3.** Investigation first found: (a) contradiction is currently silently averaged away — `applyEwmaAnswer` has no concept of "these two answers disagreed," it just EWMA-blends the values, visible only as a `stability` drop; (b) below `STABILITY_FLOOR_OVERRIDE_N` (8 answers), "never answered enough" and "answered enough but always contradictory" are numerically identical (`effectiveStability` returns literal `0` for both); (c) once the inconsistent-answerer floor (`STABILITY_INCONSISTENT_FLOOR = 0.05`) kicks in, it's indistinguishable from an axis that merely decayed to a similarly low number through ordinary aging — no flag exposed anywhere; (d) `route.ts:230-233`'s existing `wanted`-array construction (already the Phase 5 priority-list mechanism) was confirmed as the correct plug-in point, needing no downstream signature changes to `preferFreshAxes`/`composeLocalQuestionBatch`/`guardedBatch` (they already accept an arbitrary `TraitAxis[]`). **Also corrected the record:** the "`isProfileSettled` has 10 call sites across 9 files" figure repeated in several earlier decision-log entries above is wrong — direct grep confirms **8 call sites in 8 files** (`legends.tsx`, `check-milestone-badge.tsx`, `explore/route.ts`, `sage-insight.ts`, `sage-insight-spend.tsx`, `sage-title-card.tsx`, `voice/router.ts`, `voice/talk.ts`). **Built:** `isInconsistentAnswerer(row)` ([trait-stability.ts](src/lib/trait-stability.ts)) — checks the RAW stored `stability` directly (`answerCount >= 8 && stability < 0.05`), which is what actually carries the agreement signal, since a genuinely inconsistent answerer keeps raw stability pinned near 0 regardless of time while a naturally-decayed axis has HIGH raw stability that only reads low after `decayedStability` ages it at read-time — proven, not asserted, by a fixture pair that reads the SAME `effectiveStability` (both floor to 0.05) but DIFFERENT `isInconsistentAnswerer`. `hasContradictedAnswers(entries, threshold)` ([trait-history.ts](src/lib/trait-history.ts)) — reads Phase 2's `evidenceHistory`, sorts chronologically defensively (never trusts caller order), flags a genuine swing (>= `CONTRADICTION_SWING_THRESHOLD = 0.5`, a new named constant) between consecutive raw answers; independently defined from `applyEwmaAnswer`'s EWMA-vs-running-value `agreement`, a distinct and simpler concept; drops entries with an unparseable `createdAt` rather than crashing or silently misordering. `RouteQuestionsInput` gains one new optional, caller-computed field, `contradictedAxes` ([types.ts](src/lib/questions/types.ts)), spliced into `route.ts`'s `wanted` array in both the complete- and incomplete-profile branches (contradiction needs >=2 answers so it structurally can't overlap `unfilledAxes`) — confirmed byte-identical to today when omitted. **No caller computes it yet** — wiring a real caller needs `trait_history` fetched somewhere in the Questions flow, which nothing does today; that's T-04, explicitly deferred as its own decision (fetch-cost tradeoff flagged, not resolved). **Two review passes, real bugs caught and fixed:** first pass (PASS, warnings) found (a) the original `isFloored` had a dead/unreachable third check and a misleading name (a naturally-decayed axis IS floored by `effectiveStability`'s override too, yet correctly reads false from this function) — simplified and renamed to `isInconsistentAnswerer`, matching what it actually detects; (b) `route.ts`'s cached-item short-circuit (`wantedAxes` at line 193, a DIFFERENT variable than the `wanted` array T-03 targeted) was never extended with `contradictedAxes` — a landmine for T-04 (a contradiction-only request would've silently fallen through to serving an unrelated cached item, the exact bug a comment two lines below says that branch exists to prevent) — fixed; (c) the 0.5 swing threshold's doc comment misattributed it to `STABILITY_INCONSISTENT_FLOOR` (0.05, unrelated) instead of `applyEwmaAnswer`'s actual `delta/0.5` divisor — fixed, and promoted to a named exported constant; (d) no defense against a malformed `createdAt` (would silently misorder via `NaN`) — fixed with a validity filter before sorting. Second pass (PASS) confirmed all fixes hold, found only cosmetic/intentional-behavior notes (the NaN-filter's fail-quiet choice to drop a swing adjacent to a corrupt entry rather than trying to recover it is deliberate, not a regression). New assertions: `check:trait-stability` gained 2 (11 total, up from 9) — the trap-vs-decay distinction proven via identical `effectiveStability`/different `isInconsistentAnswerer`, and `hasContradictedAnswers` across empty/single/drift/swing/out-of-order/old-but-consistent/exact-boundary/malformed-date cases. `check:questions` gained 2 (37 total, up from 35) — `contradictedAxes` leads a batch ahead of the caller's own `priorityAxes`, and a byte-identical-when-omitted proof. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green, both before and after the review-driven fixes.

- 2026-09-05: **Phase 5 shipped locally (not pushed — same combined-OTA-push hold): question SELECTION now sorts by ascending `effectiveStability` within the existing freshness tiers, plus a soft redundancy-tag avoidance — the first of the six phases with real, immediate production impact**, since `stability`/`tracks` data already exists for every real user today (Phases 1-4 are all still provably inert — no content sets the new fields yet). `preferFreshAxes` ([rotation.ts](src/lib/questions/rotation.ts)) already bucketed drafts into `priority → fresh → overlap`, but within `fresh`/`overlap` order was previously arbitrary insertion order — that gap is what this phase fills. Within each tier separately (never crossing fresh into overlap, never touching `priority`, which stays a must-include set in its given order), drafts now sort by ascending stability (least-confident axis first) and a candidate whose `redundancyTags` overlap an already-chosen draft's tags is deferred to the end of ITS OWN TIER only (never dropped, backfilled if there weren't enough non-overlapping candidates — redundancy-avoidance can never shrink the batch or let an overlap draft leapfrog ahead of a fresh one). `route.ts`'s two `preferFreshAxes` call sites (inside `guardedBatch`) now pass `input.tracks ?? []`/`input.now ?? new Date()`. **Two review passes, real issues caught and fixed before commit:** first pass (PASS, warnings) found (a) the code claimed ascending-stability ties were "provably" order-preserving by citing JS's ES2019 stable-sort guarantee — true for V8, NOT something this repo can assume for Hermes (the engine React Native actually ships) — fixed with a decorate-sort-undecorate tie-break on original array index, making the no-op claim engine-independent instead of engine-dependent; (b) the new "no-op when tracks empty" test compared the new function against itself under two equivalent call shapes (tautological — a real regression could still pass) — replaced with a `legacyPreferFreshAxes` reimplementation of the actual pre-Phase-5 algorithm and a byte-identical-output assertion against it across 3 scenarios; (c) redundancy-tag deferral could let a tag-colliding FRESH draft get pushed behind an OVERLAP (recently-asked) draft, silently letting redundancy-avoidance outrank the freshness rule across the tier boundary — fixed by refactoring into a `selectTier` helper called once per tier, fully resolving fresh (including its own backfill) before overlap is ever considered. Second pass (PASS) confirmed all three fixes hold structurally (traced impossible-for-overlap-to-precede-fresh, confirmed the tie-break comparator is a strict total order, confirmed the legacy reimplementation is faithful to `git show HEAD` and that the new tier-precedence test would have failed pre-fix) and found three new, minor, inert findings, left as-is: the deferred-backfill loop doesn't add a backfilled draft's tags to `chosenTags` (asymmetric, but nothing sets `redundancyTags` yet); the legacy test helper hardcodes `recent.slice(-3)` instead of importing `QUESTIONS_AXIS_MEMORY` (test-quality only); a corrupted `lastTouched` producing NaN stability would fall back to index-order (only reachable with bad DB data). **Known, accepted limitation, not fixed (out of this phase's approved scope):** the local-bank fallback path (`composeLocalQuestionBatch` in bank.ts) calls its own internal `preferFreshAxes` without tracks, before route.ts's final tracks-aware call runs on an already-capped-5 batch — so on that path the stability sort can only REORDER the 5 axes bank.ts already chose, not truly select the least-confident ones system-wide. In practice this matters less than it sounds: the local-bank path is mainly used pre-completeness, where `unfilledAxes` priority already dominates selection anyway. New assertions: `check:questions` gained 4 (stability-ascending ordering, redundancy defers-never-drops, tier-precedence, and the legacy-algorithm equivalence check) — 35 total, up from 31. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green, both before and after the review-driven fixes.

- 2026-09-09: **T-05 shipped and pushed: ATO tokens reroll — `reroll.ts` orchestration plus real reroll UI cards on Legends, ongoing-round Questions, and Categorize.** Closes the gap left by T-04 (`wave51_ato_tokens.sql`): `spendAtoTokensLegendReroll`/`_CategoryReroll`/`_QuestionReroll` (`src/lib/ato-tokens-server.ts`) existed with zero callers. Two new migrations, both applied live to the `ato` Supabase project: `wave53_reroll_rpcs.sql` (`reroll_question_item`, `reroll_category_batch_item` — ownership-checked, unanswered-only) and a same-session review-fix migration `wave54_reroll_rpcs_fixes.sql` (adds `for update` row locking to both). Legend reroll needed no content-mutating RPC at all — Legends matching (`buildLegendView`) is already live/stateless, so reroll = compute a replacement locally then mark it shown. **Design found and fixed mid-build, before push:** the first implementation had legend reroll call `logShownVariants` and then re-derive the *whole* view — but every card on screen is already logged "seen" the instant it renders (`legends.tsx`'s load effect), so a full re-derive silently swapped every currently-shown card, not just the rerolled one, while charging 10 tokens for what a screen remount would've done for free. Fixed by lifting `catalog`/`seenIds` into `legends.tsx` state and adding `bestVariantForFigure` (`src/lib/legends/match.ts`) — reroll now finds a same-figure replacement locally (no network, no charge if none exists — most figures only have one variant today, e.g. only Da Vinci has a `v2`, wave33) and patches exactly one card. Second bug: the question-reroll precheck (`fetchBankCandidates`) checked this user's permanent exclusions but not "already used elsewhere in this same pack" — the RPC's own exclusion set — so a sparse axis could spend the token and then have the RPC find nothing, burning the 1/day cap for no effect; fixed by also fetching the pack's own bank-item ids and excluding them client-side before spending. Order for both content-swap surfaces (question, category): confirm/generate a replacement BEFORE spending (mirrors `SageInsightSpend`'s "never charge for a reroll that can't happen"); category reroll generates via the same `generateQuestionBatch`/`buildQuestionsPrompt` path `composeCategoryBatch` already uses, since `category_question_items` has no bank-pool concept. Also fixed after re-review: spend RPCs raise a Postgres exception (not `{ok:false}`) on insufficient balance — `trySpend` now catches errcode `P0040` so the "not enough tokens" copy is reachable instead of an uncaught throw; a successful reroll on any of the three surfaces now refreshes `me` so the shown ATO balance isn't stale; hardcoded reroll prices (10/1/1) replaced with `ATO_TOKEN_PRICE.*`. **Known, accepted, not fixed:** category reroll's AI generation runs before the 1/day/category cap check (so a repeat tap on an exhausted day burns a small AI call before saying "Already rerolled today") — low-frequency, self-limiting by the cap itself, same risk class as other documented AI-cost tradeoffs in this codebase. Two review passes (first: 3 critical findings, all fixed as above; second, after fixes: PASS, only "should fix, can push" warnings, the reachable ones also fixed same session). New `check:reroll` (15 assertions) added to the offline gate. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 75 offline checks green. Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified. Files: `supabase/migrations/wave53_reroll_rpcs.sql`, `supabase/migrations/wave54_reroll_rpcs_fixes.sql`, `src/lib/questions/reroll.ts`, `src/lib/legends/match.ts`, `src/components/legend-card.tsx`, `src/app/(tabs)/legends.tsx`, `src/components/questions-fold.tsx`, `src/lib/ato-tokens-server.ts`, `scripts/reroll-check.ts`.

- 2026-09-10: **Fixed: `OngoingRoundFold`'s `load()`/`start()` Sentry captures were never flushed, plus a `PRE_LAUNCH_DEV` raw-error display added; the 50th-question/`legends_unlocked` interference hypothesis was investigated and ruled out.** Emci reported an ongoing-round submit failure and asked to (1) fix Sentry delivery, (2) add a dev-only raw error display, (3) check whether crossing the 50th answered question (the `legends_unlocked` celebration firing `claimFullProfileComplete()`, per the 2026-09-09 entry below) could interfere with starting an ongoing round right after. **(1)/(2):** `Sentry.captureException(err)` in both catch blocks ([questions-fold.tsx](src/components/questions-fold.tsx), `load()` ~line 588, `start()` ~line 617) had no `await Sentry.flush()` after it — unlike `sendFloorTestError` in [sentry.ts:23-33](src/lib/sentry.ts:23), the one other place in the codebase that captures-then-flushes. Added the same bare `await Sentry.flush()` (no timeout arg, matching the existing precedent) to both. Also added `errorDetail: string | null` state, set to `String(err)` in both catches, rendered as small secondary text under the existing generic error copy, gated `{errorDetail && PRE_LAUNCH_DEV ? (...) : null}` — same conditional-render shape used in `sage-title-card.tsx:204`, `roll-item-body.tsx`, `full-profile-fold.tsx`. **(3) Ruled out, not just unconfirmed:** `claim_full_profile_complete` and `claim_ongoing_round_complete` (both wave52) take the *same* per-user `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, so concurrent calls serialize rather than collide; their `ato_token_events` unique constraints are disjoint (`reason='full_profile_complete'` vs `(user_id, pack_id)` for `reason='ongoing_round_complete'`); `claim_full_profile_complete` touches no `question_packs` row; and no code path automatically starts a new ongoing-round pack immediately after either claim call (`start()`/`load()` are user/UI-triggered only). No shared-table, shared-lock, or trigger collision exists between the two. The two un-flushed Sentry captures are the far more credible explanation for a silent, undiagnosed submit failure. Gate: typecheck + lint run after the change. Files: `src/components/questions-fold.tsx`, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`.

- 2026-09-10: **T-06 shipped and pushed: Legend candidates — a root-only AI-generation + review-approval pipeline that fills the 8 of 12 archetypes with zero Legend content today.** No new pool table: `legend_variants` already had the exact right gate (`fact_checked`, false until a human verifies it — wave32), so an AI candidate lands directly in the existing tables (`legend_figures`/`legend_variants`/`legend_archetypes`) with `fact_checked=false`, `source='ai'` (new column, wave55) — invisible to `fetchLegendCatalog`/matching/reroll until a root approve flips it. Two migrations applied live: `wave55_legend_candidates.sql` (the `source` column; `claim_legend_generation()` mirroring `claim_roll_generation`'s own-cap-own-quota-key pattern; `insert_legend_candidate`; `approve_legend_variant`/`reject_legend_variant`) and a same-session fix migration `wave56_legend_candidates_fixes.sql`. New `src/lib/legends/{prompt,parse,generate,generate-store}.ts` and a root-only review section in `dev-lab.tsx` (`LegendCandidatesReview`) — lists the zero-coverage archetypes with a Generate button each, lists pending candidates with Approve/Reject. Generation prompt reuses `docs/legends-content-spec.md`'s approved format + its 4 reference samples as few-shot verbatim.

**Two review rounds, two real bugs caught and fixed before push — the second one self-inflicted by the first fix:** Round 1 found `insert_legend_candidate` had **no `require_root()` at all** — `security definer` + granted to `authenticated`, writing into tables wave25/wave32 deliberately revoke all direct client writes on. Any signed-in user could call the RPC directly (bypassing the dev-lab UI) and plant an attacker-authored candidate that looks legitimate in the root review queue — a review-queue-poisoning path, the single worst thing this feature could do wrong. Fixed in `wave56`: `require_root()` added to both `insert_legend_candidate` and `claim_legend_generation`; `approve_legend_variant`'s WHERE clause tightened to `and fact_checked = false` (was a silent no-op-succeed on an already-approved row). Round 1 also flagged (and round-1-fix addressed) a stale migration comment claiming `require_root()` checks `handle='emci'` — false since `wave34_root_is_column.sql`, which moved it to `me.is_root` (flipped for `emci2`) — corrected in `wave56`'s header and in dev-lab copy, and a minor tightening: `parseLegendCandidate` gained an `expectedArchetypeName` param rejecting a response whose "Energy:" line names the wrong archetype. **That tightening was itself broken and caught in round 2**: `archetype_defs.formal_name` always carries a leading "The" (e.g. "The Architect", wave26), but every approved reference sample and every shipped legend drops it in the Energy line ("Architect Energy:", never "The Architect Energy:") — the new guard compared against the untrimmed form, so a genuinely correct response would have been silently rejected, burning a day's 5-generation quota for nothing every time. Root cause was already latent in the original prompt instruction (`"${archetype.formalName} Energy:"`, untrimmed) contradicting its own few-shot examples — the strict guard just made the pre-existing inconsistency load-bearing. Fixed with a shared `energyLineName()` helper (`prompt.ts`) used by both the instruction text and the parser guard, so the two can't drift apart again; source-locked in `check:legend-candidates` (7 new assertions this round, 16 total) so a `formal_name` used raw anywhere in this path fails the gate. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 76 offline checks green after every round. **Known, accepted:** `fetchPendingLegendCandidates` reads via the existing open `authenticated` select policy on `legend_variants` (same inherited trust model as the rest of the content catalog, not a new gap — see wave53's reroll entry for the same pattern) — a non-root user could technically read pending drafts directly, just never approve/reject them. Not yet device-verified (this is a root-only dev-lab tool, not user-facing).

- 2026-09-05: **T-04 shipped locally (not pushed — same combined-OTA-push hold): `questions-fold.tsx` is now a real caller of Phase 6's contradiction detection — the first genuinely live-impact change in the whole 6-phase project (Phases 1-4 and 6's T-01/T-02/T-03 are all still provably inert; Phase 5 already went live via `tracks`/`stability`, which always existed for real users).** Investigation first confirmed there was nothing to reuse: `trait_history` has no shared cache anywhere in the app (grepped every context — `me-context.tsx` and 18 other `createContext` files, none carry it); the one existing reader, `FullProfileFold`'s "How this has shifted", fetches it fresh on its own mount, same as every `trait_tracks` reader in the app (9 separate screens, each its own fetch) — "fetch fresh per screen" is this codebase's established pattern, not a gap to fix. Row growth was confirmed small and bounded (every insert traces to one choke point, `persistMergedTraits` in `me.ts`, triggered only by direct user actions, never a timer; documented caps put a heavy 6-month user in the low hundreds of rows per axis at most) — no windowing/limit needed. **Built:** `contradictedAxesFrom(history)` ([trait-history.ts](src/lib/trait-history.ts)) — the real consumer of `hasContradictedAnswers`, iterating every `TRAIT_AXES` entry, filtering each axis's history to report-track only via `trackKindForSource` (gut-call never counts, same convention as `effectiveStability`/`isProfileSettled`). `questions-fold.tsx` gained a `useEffect`/`useState` fetching `trait_history` keyed on the whole `me` object (matching `load`'s own existing dependency convention — `useMe`'s `refresh()` always returns a new object, so this re-fires after an answer lands) and a `useMemo`-computed `contradictedAxes` now passed into the `routeQuestions` call. `intake-sweep.tsx` deliberately left unwired: its `routeQuestionSweep`/`composeLocalSweep` path serves all 16 axes unconditionally in one pass with no priority/ordering concept at all (confirmed by reading `composeLocalSweep` directly), so wiring contradiction-awareness there would need a bigger, separate change, not a trivial one — documented at `sweep.ts`. **Two review passes, real bugs caught and fixed:** first pass (PASS, warnings) found (a) no test locked the one line making this live (`contradictedAxes` in the `routeQuestions` call) — a source-assertion added; (b) a comment claimed the fetch "refreshes after an answer lands" without mentioning `pick()`'s subsequent `load()` still runs against the PRE-answer value from that render's closure (the fetch is async) — a real one-batch lag, comment corrected; (c) a comment undersold the fetch as "bounded by actual answering" when it's genuinely unbounded (no `.limit()`), just matching an existing precedent (`full-profile-fold.tsx` does the same) — reworded; (d) the test fixture's report-track filter case used an axis where ALL rows were `self_game`, which could pass for the wrong reason — replaced with a mixed-source fixture (two consistent report values with a `self_game` outlier sandwiched between them) that would produce an observable false positive if the filter broke. **Second pass caught that fix (a) was itself broken**: the added assertion (`assert.match(fold, /contradictedAxes/)`) was a substring match that also matched `contradictedAxesFrom`'s import/declaration, so it would still pass even with the actual wiring line deleted — empirically verified this myself (deleted the wiring line, watched the old assertion still pass, confirmed the fix, restored the line, confirmed the new anchored regex genuinely fails without it before restoring). Fixed by anchoring the regex to the literal `routeQuestions` call-site pattern (`tracks,\s*\n\s*contradictedAxes,`) instead of a bare substring; also corrected a wrong cross-reference to `intake-sweep.tsx` "already documenting" the same one-batch lag (it doesn't — removed the false claim). New assertions: `check:trait-stability` gained 1 (12 total, up from 11) — `contradictedAxesFrom` against a fixture proving the report-track filter with a genuine false-positive-if-broken case. `check:questions` gained 1 (38 total, up from 37) — the anchored source-lock on the live wiring, empirically verified to fail when the wiring is removed. Gate: typecheck + lint + all 50 offline checks green, both before and after the review-driven fixes.

- 2026-09-09: **Core loop redesign T-04 shipped and pushed (commit `75e39f0`): a brand-new "ATO tokens" currency, fully separate from Sage's Notes economy (`me.tokens`/`token_events`).** Per `docs/CORE_LOOP_REDESIGN_PLAN.md` §5 (do not confuse with the unrelated trait-redesign "T-04" logged 2026-09-05 above — task numbering is reused across the two separate plan documents). **Schema (waves 49-52, all applied live to production this session):** `wave49_question_bank_pool.sql` + `wave50_ongoing_round_prep.sql` were also unapplied prerequisites (question_bank_pool seed, question_packs.kind/completed_at, insert_ongoing_round_pack) — applied first since claim_ongoing_round_complete needs those columns. `wave51_ato_tokens.sql`: `me.ato_tokens` balance column with its own client-write-guard trigger (`me_ato_tokens_guard`, using a config flag `ato.allow_ato_token_write` deliberately separate from Notes' own `ato.allow_token_write` so the two economies' guards can never piggyback on each other in one transaction) — same pattern as `me_tokens_guard`/`me_guard_is_root`; new `ato_token_events` ledger (separate table, not a shared reason string on `token_events`, per the plan's explicit resolution); 5 RPCs: `claim_full_profile_complete()` (+21 once ever), `claim_ongoing_round_complete(pack_id)` (+21 once per pack — keyed on `question_packs.id`, not a `round_number` column like wave45's still-unshipped `round_complete` speculated, since nothing in this codebase actually numbers rounds), `spend_ato_tokens_legend_reroll()` (-10, capped 1/day), `spend_ato_tokens_category_reroll(category_id)` (-1, capped 1/day/category), `spend_ato_tokens_question_reroll(question_item_id)` (-1, capped 1/day/question-slot) — caps confirmed by emci as already-decided spec (finalized product plan item 5), not a judgment call. **`reviewer` subagent pass found 4 CRITICAL issues in wave51, all fixed same-session via `wave52_ato_tokens_fixes.sql` (create-or-replace/alter over the already-live wave51, same convention as wave47 patching wave46 — never edit an applied migration in place):** (1) `ato_token_events.pack_id`/`question_item_id` were `on delete set null`, which combined with this table's own reason-matching CHECK constraints made `delete-account` abort — deleting a user cascades to `question_packs`, whose FK action tried to null `pack_id` mid-cascade and tripped the CHECK before the row's own `user_id`-cascade delete could run; fixed to `on delete cascade` on both FKs. (2) `claim_ongoing_round_complete` treated a SKIPPED item as resolved (`answered_option is null and skipped_at is null`), but `skip_rest_question_pack` (wave18) has no kind filter — a user could skip an entire 25-item round and still claim +21 for zero real answers; fixed to require `answered_option is not null` for every item, never consulting `skipped_at`. (3) No floor on pack size meant a 1-item ongoing-round pack with 1 real answer would satisfy "every item answered" and mint +21 repeatably — fixed by also requiring exactly 25 `question_items` rows per pack (composeOngoingRound's only real shape). (4) `claim_full_profile_complete` did zero server-side verification — any authenticated caller got +21 once, no matter how many questions they'd actually answered; fixed to require >= 50 `trait_history` rows with `source = 'self_situation'` (the write source every bank-question answer path uses — intake, Infinite Questions, and the ongoing round all call `updateTraits(..., 'self_situation', ...)`) — a real floor, not a byte-identical reimplementation of the client's tiered `bankTotalProgress` counting, but sufficient to close "zero answers, free tokens." All 4 fixes verified live via direct SQL query (FK defs, function source text) after emci applied wave52 through the SQL editor. **Client:** `src/lib/ato-tokens.ts` + `ato-tokens-server.ts`, deliberately separate modules from `tokens.ts`/`tokens-server.ts` (checked: `check:ato-tokens` asserts zero cross-references either direction). **Wiring:** `legends.tsx`'s existing one-time `legends_unlocked` Q50-crossing celebration now also fires `claimFullProfileComplete()` (fire-and-forget; the RPC's own once-ever unique index is the real dedup). `questions-fold.tsx`'s `OngoingRoundFold.pick()` fires `claimOngoingRoundCompleteQuiet(pack.id)` when the just-answered item was the last unanswered one, plus a retry-on-load in the same fold's `load()` for a lost fire-and-forget call (RPC dedupes on pack id, so a redundant claim is a harmless no-op). Also added `ato_tokens` to the `Me` type/parse in `me.ts` (caught in review — the column existed in the DB and in `ato-tokens.ts`'s helpers but was never read into the app's own `Me` row, making those helpers unreachable dead code). New checks `check:wave51`/`check:wave52`/`check:ato-tokens` (22 assertions total), part of the offline gate — **`check:ota-gate` green at 74/74** before push. No reroll UI built (Legends/Categorize cards, `reroll.ts` itself) — that is future work (T-09/10/14 in the plan); this task only builds the schema/RPCs/client plumbing those will call.

- 2026-09-10: **Questions screen UI polish batch (5 fixes) + Full Profile restructured into a flat 5-per-page book pager + best-effort ongoing-round diagnostics.** Ran concurrently with another session's own fix to the same `OngoingRoundFold` Sentry captures (see the entry directly above this one, same date — that session added `Sentry.flush()`/`errorDetail`/`PRE_LAUNCH_DEV` raw-error display and ruled out a `legends_unlocked`/`claim_full_profile_complete` interference hypothesis; the two changesets merged cleanly, confirmed by a green gate after both landed). No live Sentry dashboard access in this session, so the ongoing-round bug's root cause is still not confirmed — this task only hedges. **UI fixes, all in `questions-fold.tsx` unless noted:** (1) question prompt text bumped from ThemedText's shared 16/24 "default" to an 18/26 local style override (rotating flow + `OngoingRoundFold`) and a 16/22 override in the new book pager — never touched the shared `ThemedText` type, so no other screen resized. (2) The busy-dim (`busy && styles.disabled`) previously applied even to the just-picked/highlighted option, so a tap looked like "selected AND broken" for the round-trip duration; excluded the picked option from the dim in both the rotating flow and `OngoingRoundFold` (mirrors the fix `CategoryPagedQuestions` already had from `afd6365`) — `OngoingRoundFold` gained its own `pickedIndex` state since it previously had no per-option highlight at all. Root latency itself (multiple serial awaits per answer: item write, trait write, token grant, refresh, and for the rotating flow a full batch reload) is unchanged — flagged as a separate, larger tradeoff, not fixed here. (3) Auto-scroll-to-next-unanswered (`scrollToNextUnanswered`, `SCROLL_TARGET_OFFSET`) deleted outright from `category-paged-questions.tsx`, plus its now-dead `scrollViewRef` plumbing through `questions-fold.tsx` and `src/app/(tabs)/intake-sweep.tsx` (the host screen's own `scrollRef` stays — still used by `intake-sweep.tsx`'s own skip-to-top). (4) Confirmed the "Answered" stamp already had no entrance animation — the only motion was the busy-dim flash fixed in (2) — carried over unchanged into the book pager. (5) `CategoryPagedQuestions` (confirmed as the exact Full Profile component, per emci's ask to verify before starting) rebuilt from one-category-per-screen Back/Skip/Next paging into a flat, axis-order, 5-questions-per-page book pager, Back/Next Page only (no Skip — nothing to skip in a flat list); `category-page-position.ts` repurposed to store a page-index string instead of a category id (same generic string-storage shape, just what's put in it changed — old stored category-id values parse to `NaN` and are safely ignored, not a migration); `answered-option-storage.ts` reused unchanged (still keyed by row key). **Ongoing-round diagnostics (best-effort, T-06):** `load()`/`start()` wrapped in the same 25s `withTimeout` `QuestionsFold.load()` already uses (a hang previously never surfaced as a Sentry event at all); `Sentry.captureException` calls on all three catch sites (load/start/answer) gained scoped `tags: {stage}` (`answer` also gets `extra: {packId, itemId}`); the answer-path catch (`pick()`) previously had NO Sentry capture, no flush, and no user-visible signal at all on a failed save (silently un-picked and stopped) — reviewer flagged this as "the exact blind spot T-06 exists to close" and it's now captured, flushed (`Sentry.flush()`, fire-and-forget, non-blocking — this SDK's `flush()` takes no timeout arg, confirmed against `@sentry/react-native`'s own `.d.ts`, not `@sentry/core`'s which does), and shown as a small "Couldn't save that answer. Try again." note. Also fixed in review: the other session's `await Sentry.flush()` calls in `load()`/`start()` ran *before* `setErrorKind`/`setErrorDetail`, so a stalled flush would leave the user on an endless spinner instead of the error card — reordered to set error state first, flush second and non-blocking. **Reviewer pass (PASS, no criticals):** found and fixed 3 warnings — the two flush-ordering issues above, the missing answer-path capture/signal above, and a position-persist effect in the new book pager that lost its old `!current`-equivalent guard (would write `pageIndex=0` even with zero rows loaded — unreachable today since the Full Profile bank is never empty, but a live trap for the future "questions-stack" caller this component is explicitly generic for) — fixed with an `allRows.length === 0` guard. Updated `scripts/category-paged-questions-check.ts` (rewritten for the new pager's flat/paged shape), `scripts/questions-check.ts` (a few source-lock assertions updated for the new text/structure), `scripts/ongoing-round-save-check.ts` (two regexes updated for the `withTimeout` wrapping). Gate: typecheck + lint + all 74 offline checks green, before and after the review-driven fixes. Not yet OTA'd, not device-verified — dev-test login (`@atodev` / the password documented in `docs/GOTCHAS.md`) failed against the local web preview during this session (rejected, possibly rotated since that doc was written), so this batch is verified by source review + the full offline gate only, not a live click-through. Files: `src/components/questions-fold.tsx`, `src/components/category-paged-questions.tsx`, `src/app/(tabs)/intake-sweep.tsx`, `src/lib/questions/category-page-position.ts`, `src/lib/questions/answered-option-storage.ts`, `scripts/category-paged-questions-check.ts`, `scripts/questions-check.ts`, `scripts/ongoing-round-save-check.ts`.

- 2026-09-15: **Workflow change — SUPERSEDES the "one gate, one reviewer, at
  the end" rule above (Sep 14 entry) wherever it says to run `check:ota-gate`
  (emci explicit).** From now on, do **NOT** run `npm run check:ota-gate`
  after a fix or a build — not once at the end, not per-card, not ever
  automatically. Emci runs the full gate himself, on his own schedule.
  Narrower verification (`npm run typecheck`, lint on touched files, or the
  one obviously-relevant `check:*` script for what changed) is still fine and
  still expected — this is specifically about the full aggregate gate command,
  not about skipping verification altogether. The reviewer subagent pass at
  the end of a task is unaffected by this and still happens as before.
  **The one standing exception: always ask emci before running
  `npm run ota:publish` / publishing any OTA update** — that part of the
  existing discipline does not change.

- 2026-09-15: **Fixed: removed the "A faster pass" full sweep from Questions;
  the bank list now opens expanded by default.** Root cause/scope: emci asked
  to delete `IntakeSweep` entirely (the 50 bank questions already cover the
  same ground) and to stop requiring a tap to reveal the question list.
  Deleted `src/components/intake-sweep.tsx` and `src/lib/questions/sweep.ts`
  outright (not unmounted), trimmed their sweep-only exports out of
  `src/lib/questions/local.ts` (`QUESTIONS_SWEEP_SIZE`,
  `INTAKE_SWEEP_COPY_REVIEWED`, `composeLocalSweep`, `unansweredSweep`) and the
  now-dead `keepGuardedDrafts` import; every shared bank/rotation export
  (`axisVariant`, `bankDraftFor`, `composeLocalQuestionBatch`, etc.) is
  untouched. **Real conflict caught mid-fix (reviewer + emci confirmed):**
  making `QuestionsFold` expand via its existing `defaultOpen` prop would have
  auto-fired `handleOpen()` → `routeQuestions` on every mount — a real paid AI
  batch for anyone who has finished the 50-question bank, with no tap behind
  it, reopening the exact bug ISOLATION_PLAN §7 Card D had removed from this
  same file hours earlier the same day. Resolved (emci's pick): `QuestionsFold`
  now renders `alwaysOpen` — the bank list (`PagedQuestions`) shows expanded
  immediately with zero backend reach — while its separate "Tell Sage more"
  5-item rotation, the part that can reach a paid batch, keeps its own new
  explicit "Tell Sage more" press (`QUESTIONS_LOAD_MORE`) instead of loading
  with the rest. `check:no-auto-ai` (the fixpoint-tracing effect check, not a
  text regex) confirms nothing in the file's effects can reach a model call.
  Also fixed a dangling `readFileSync` on the deleted `sweep.ts` in
  `ai-provider-check.ts` (would have crashed `check:ai`), and corrected an
  inaccurate coverage claim in `badges-check.ts` (QuestionsFold's own,
  separate `skipRest()` has no test coverage — that gap is pre-existing, not
  opened by this change). `check:questions`/`wave19`/`badges`/
  `copy-review-badges`/`ai`/`no-auto-ai`/`full-profile`/`milestones` all green
  (targeted, not the full gate — see workflow-change entry above).
